\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(Q-Y_{\ell}(Q))^{p}\overline{(Q-Y_{\ell}(Q))^{p}}{\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)}]=\frac{1}{Z_{{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}}\sum_{{\bf i}_{0}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}_{0},{\mathcal{G}}){\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)}R_{{\bf i}_{0}}\right]+\OO(\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}]),

where
Z_{{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}=Nd
.
The above expression is in the form of
Proposition

3.9
, and we can start to use
Proposition

3.9
and
Proposition

3.10
to iterate. Each step of the iteration, we will either get an extra factor
(d-1)^{-\ell/2}\leqslant N^{-{\mathfrak{c}}/256}
as in (
3.16
); or as in (
3.17
), we get an extra factor in the form (
3.13
). For
z\in\mathbf{D}
, these factors are much smaller than
N^{-{\mathfrak{c}}/256}
, thanks to
Theorem

2.4
. Therefore after a finite number of iterations (at most
256/{\mathfrak{c}}
), the terms are all bounded by
\OO(\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}])
. This finishes the proof of (
3.2
).
∎

3.4
Proof of
Theorem

1.1
and
Theorem

1.2

In this section, we prove
Theorem

1.1
and
Theorem

1.2
using
Corollary

3.3
as input.
We recall the following estimates from
[
24
, (6.12), (6.13)]
and (
2.9
), which give that for
z=E+\mathrm{i}\eta
,
\kappa:=\min\{|E-2|,|E+2|\}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(m_{\rm sc}(z))|=|1-m_{\rm sc}(z)^{2\ell+2}|,\quad|1-m_{sc}^{2}(z)|\asymp\sqrt{\kappa+\eta},\\
&\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m_{d}(z)]\asymp\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}&|E|\leqslant 2,\\
\eta/\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}&|E|>2.\end{array}\right.\end{split}

(3.19)

Since
|m_{\rm sc}(z)|\leqslant 1
, we can take
({\mathfrak{c}}/128)\log_{d-1}N\leqslant\ell\leqslant({\mathfrak{c}}/64)\log_{d-1}N
such that
|1+m_{sc}^{2}(z)+m_{sc}^{4}(z)+\cdots+m_{sc}^{2\ell}(z)|\gtrsim 1
.
The following stability Proposition states that if
|m(z)-X_{\ell}(Q(z))|
and
|Q(z)-Y_{\ell}(Q(z))|
are small, so are
|Q(z)-m_{\rm sc}(z)|
and
|m(z)-m_{d}(z)|
.

Proposition 3.11
.

Fix a
z=E+\mathrm{i}\eta\in\mathbf{D}
, with
\kappa=\min\{|E-2|,|E+2|\}
, and a
d
-regular graph
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z)
. Assume that

\displaystyle|Q(z)-m(z)|,|m(z)-m_{d}(z)|\leqslant\min\left\{\frac{1}{N^{\mathfrak{c}}},\frac{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}{\log N}\right\},

and
there exists a small
0<\delta<1
so that,

\displaystyle|m(z)-X_{\ell}(Q(z))|,|Q(z)-Y_{\ell}(Q(z))|=:\delta\leqslant\frac{\kappa+\eta}{\log N}.

Then for
N
large enough,

\displaystyle\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z))|Q(z)-m_{\rm sc}(z)|,\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z))|m(z)-m_{d}(z)|=\OO\left(\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta+\delta}}\right),

(3.20)

where the implicit constant is independent of
N
.

Proof of
Proposition

3.11
.

In the proof, for the simplicity of the notations, we omit the dependence on
z
. We have from (
2.8
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)&=(Q-m_{sc})-(Y_{\ell}(Q)-m_{sc})\\
&=(1-m_{sc}^{2\ell+2})(Q-m_{sc})-m_{sc}^{2\ell+3}\left(\frac{1-m_{sc}^{2\ell+2}}{1-m_{sc}^{2}}\right)(Q-m_{sc})^{2}+O(\ell^{2}|Q-m_{sc}|^{3}).\end{split}

(3.21)

We consider the quadratic equation
ax^{2}+bx+c=0
, with

\displaystyle\begin{split}&a=-m_{sc}^{2\ell+3}\left(\frac{1-m_{sc}^{2\ell+2}}{1-m_{sc}^{2}}\right)+\OO\left(\ell^{2}|Q-m_{sc}|\right),\\
&b=1-m_{sc}^{2\ell+2},\quad c=Y_{\ell}(Q)-Q,\quad|c|=\delta.\end{split}

(3.22)

We recall that by our choice of
\ell
, it holds
|1+(m_{\rm sc})^{2}+(m_{\rm sc})^{4}+\cdots+(m_{\rm sc})^{2\ell}|\gtrsim 1
.
It follows that
|a|\gtrsim|m_{\rm sc}|^{2\ell+3}
.
\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}\lesssim|1-m_{sc}^{2}|\lesssim|b|\lesssim\ell|1-m_{sc}^{2}|\lesssim\ell\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}
and
|b/a|=|1-m_{sc}^{2}|/|m_{sc}|^{2\ell+3}\gtrsim\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}
.

By our assumption
\delta\ll\kappa+\eta
, then
|ac/b^{2}|\lesssim|c|/(\kappa+\eta)\ll 1
.
The two roots of
ax^{2}+bx+c=0
satisfies:
|x_{1}|=|b/a|+\OO(|c/b|)\asymp|b/a|\gtrsim\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}
and

\displaystyle|x_{2}|=\OO(|c/b|)\lesssim\frac{|c|}{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}\lesssim\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta+\delta}}.

(3.23)

Since
|Q-m_{\rm sc}|\ll\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}
, it is necessary that

\displaystyle|Q-m_{\rm sc}|=|x_{2}|\lesssim\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta+\delta}}.

The first statement in (
3.20
) follows.
To prove the second statement in (
3.20
), thanks to Proposition
2.5
, we rewrite
m-m_{d}
as

\displaystyle\begin{split}m-m_{d}&=m-X_{\ell}(Q)+X_{\ell}(Q)-m_{d}\\
&=m-X_{\ell}(Q)+\frac{d}{d-1}(m_{d})^{2}(m_{sc})^{2\ell}|Q-m_{sc}|+\OO\left(\ell|Q-m_{sc}|^{2}\right).\end{split}

(3.24)

It follows from plugging (
3.20
) into (
3.24
), we get

\displaystyle\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|m-m_{d}|\lesssim\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta+\delta}}.

∎

Lemma 3.12
.

Fix a
z=E+\mathrm{i}\eta\in\mathbf{D}
, with
\kappa=\min\{|E-2|,|E+2|\}
. If there exists some deterministic control parameter
\Lambda(z)
such that the prior estimate holds

\displaystyle\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z))|m(z)-m_{d}(z)|,\quad\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z))|Q(z)-m_{\rm sc}(z)|\prec\Lambda(z)\leqslant\frac{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}{N^{2{\mathfrak{c}}}}.

(3.25)

Then

1.

If
|E|\leqslant 2
, the following holds

\displaystyle\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z))|Q(z)-m_{\rm sc}(z)|,\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z))|m(z)-m_{d}(z)|\prec\frac{N^{2{\mathfrak{c}}}}{N\eta}.

(3.26)

2.

If
|E|\geqslant 2
, the following holds

\displaystyle\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z))|Q(z)-m_{\rm sc}(z)|,\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z))|m(z)-m_{d}(z)|\prec N^{2{\mathfrak{c}}}\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{\eta(\kappa+\eta)}}+\frac{1}{(N\eta)^{2}\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}\right).

(3.27)

Proof.

We recall the moment bounds of
Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)
amd
m-X_{\ell}(Q)
from
Corollary

3.3
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|^{2p}]\lesssim N^{4p{\mathfrak{c}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\left(\frac{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m]|1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(Q)|}}{N\eta}+\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m]}{N\eta}+|Q-m_{\rm sc}|^{2}\right)^{2p}\right],\\
&\mathbb{E}[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|m-X_{\ell}(Q)|^{2p}]\lesssim N^{4p{\mathfrak{c}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\left(\frac{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m]|1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(Q)|}}{N\eta}+\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m]}{N\eta}+|Q-m_{\rm sc}|^{2}\right)^{2p}\right].\end{split}

(3.28)

By our assumption (
3.25
), we recall that condition on
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m(z)]\lesssim\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m_{d}]+|m-m_{d}|\prec\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m_{d}]+\Lambda,\\
&|1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(Q)|=|1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(m_{\rm sc})|+\OO(|Q-m_{\rm sc}|)\prec\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}+\Lambda.\end{split}

(3.29)

Plugging (
3.25
) into the first statement in (
3.28
), we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\mathbb{E}[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|^{2p}]\\
&\prec N^{4p{\mathfrak{c}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\left(\frac{\sqrt{(\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m_{d}]+\Lambda)(|1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(m_{\rm sc})|+\Lambda)}}{N\eta}+\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m_{d}]+\Lambda}{N\eta}+\Lambda^{2}\right)^{2p}\right],\end{split}

(3.30)

Thus the Markov’s inequality leads to the following

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|&\prec N^{2{\mathfrak{c}}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{(\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m_{d}]+\Lambda)(|1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(m_{\rm sc})|+\Lambda)}}{N\eta}+\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m_{d}]+\Lambda}{N\eta}+\Lambda^{2}\right)\\
&\prec N^{2{\mathfrak{c}}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m_{d}]\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}+\sqrt{\Lambda\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}}{N\eta}+\Lambda^{2}\right),\end{split}

(3.31)

where in the last line we used (
3.19
) that
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m_{d}]\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}
,
|1-Y^{\prime}(m_{\rm sc})|\prec\ell\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}
, and
\Lambda\prec\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}
.
Similarly it follows from the second statement in (
3.28
) that

\displaystyle\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|m-X_{\ell}(Q)|\prec N^{2{\mathfrak{c}}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m_{d}]\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}+\sqrt{\Lambda\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}}{N\eta}+\Lambda^{2}\right).

(3.32)

Using (
3.31
) and (
3.32
) as input,
Proposition

3.11
implies that

\displaystyle\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|Q-m_{\rm sc}|,\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|m-m_{d}|\prec N^{2{\mathfrak{c}}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m_{d}]\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}+\sqrt{\Lambda\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}}{N\eta\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}+\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}\right).

(3.33)

If
|E|\geqslant 2
, then
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m_{d}]\asymp\eta/\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}
, and (
3.33
) simplifies to

\displaystyle\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|Q-m_{\rm sc}|,\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|m-m_{d}|\prec N^{2{\mathfrak{c}}}\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{\eta(\kappa+\eta)}}+\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{N\eta(\kappa+\eta)^{1/4}}+\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}\right).

(3.34)

We can take the new
\Lambda
as the righthand side of (
3.34
), by iterating (
3.34
), we get

\displaystyle\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|Q-m_{\rm sc}|,\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|m-m_{d}|\prec\frac{N^{2{\mathfrak{c}}}}{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{\eta}}+\frac{1}{(N\eta)^{2}}\right).

(3.35)

This finishes the proof of (
3.26
).

If
|E|\leqslant 2
, then
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m_{d}]\asymp\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}
and
\Lambda(w)\prec\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}
, (
3.33
) simplifies to

\displaystyle\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|Q-m_{\rm sc}|,\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|m-m_{d}|\prec N^{2{\mathfrak{c}}}\left(\frac{1}{N\eta}+\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}\right).

(3.36)

Again, we can take the new
\Lambda
as the righthand side of (
3.36
), by iterating (
3.36
), we get

\displaystyle\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|Q-m_{\rm sc}|,\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|m-m_{d}|\prec\frac{N^{2{\mathfrak{c}}}}{N\eta}.

(3.37)

This finishes the proof of (
3.27
).

∎

Proof of
Theorem

1.2
.

We take a lattice grid

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}=\{E+\mathrm{i}\eta\in\mathbf{D}:E\in N^{-2}\mathbb{Z},\eta\in N^{-2}\mathbb{Z}\}.

For
{\mathcal{G}}\in\overline{\Omega}
and
z\in\mathbf{D}
,
m(z),Q(z),m_{\rm sc}(z),m_{d}(z)
are all Lipschitz with Lipschitz constant at most
\OO(N)
.
Thus if we can show that for any
z\in\mathbf{L}

\displaystyle\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\overline{\Omega})|Q(z)-m_{\rm sc}(z)|,\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\overline{\Omega})|m(z)-m_{d}(z)|\prec\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{N^{2{\mathfrak{c}}}}{N\eta},&-2\leqslant E\leqslant 2,\\
\frac{N^{2{\mathfrak{c}}}}{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}\left(\frac{1}{N\eta^{1/2}}+\frac{1}{(N\eta)^{2}}\right),&|E|\geqslant 2.\end{array}\right.

then
Theorem

1.2
follows.

First for
z\in\mathbf{L}
with
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\geqslant 1
,
Theorem

2.4
implies that

\displaystyle\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\overline{\Omega})|m(z)-m_{d}(z)|,\quad\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\overline{\Omega})|Q(z)-m_{\rm sc}(z)|\prec\frac{1}{N^{2{\mathfrak{c}}}}\leqslant\frac{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}{N^{2{\mathfrak{c}}}}.

This verifies the assumption (
3.25
) in
Lemma

3.12
. We conclude that (
3.4
) holds for
z\in\mathbf{L}
with
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\geqslant 1
. Then inductively we can show that if (
3.4
) holds for
z\in\mathbf{L}
with
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\geqslant(k+1)/N^{2}
, then it also holds for
z\in\mathbf{L}
with
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\geqslant k/N^{2}
. More precisely, for
z=E+\mathrm{i}\eta\in\mathbf{L}
with
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\geqslant k/N^{2}
, we denote
z^{\prime}=z+(\mathrm{i}/N^{2})\in\mathbf{L}
. By the inductive hypothesis, and that the Lipschitz constants of
m(z),m_{d}(z)
are at most
\OO(N)

\displaystyle\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\overline{\Omega})|m(z)-m_{d}(z)|=\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\overline{\Omega})|m(z^{\prime})-m_{d}(z^{\prime})|+\OO(1/N)\prec\frac{N^{2{\mathfrak{c}}}}{N\eta}\leqslant\frac{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}{N^{2{\mathfrak{c}}}}.

Lemma

3.12
implies that (
3.4
) holds for
z
. This finishes the induction and
Theorem

1.2
.

∎

Proof of
Theorem

1.1
.

The optimal rigidity (
1.3
) follows from a standard argument using the Stieltjes transform (
1.2
) estimate as an input, see
[
24
, Section 11]
. For didactic purposes, we show here how to show that
\lambda_{2}\leqslant 2+N^{-2/3+2{\mathfrak{a}}}
holds with overwhelmingly high probability on
\overline{\Omega}
. It has been proven in
[
40
, Theorem 1.3]
that with high probability on
\overline{\Omega}
, it holds

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}\leqslant 2+N^{-\oo(1)}.

(3.40)

In the following we show that with high probability, there is no eigenvalue on the interval
[2+N^{-2/3+2{\mathfrak{a}}},2+{\mathfrak{a}}]
. It, together with (
3.40
), implies that
\lambda_{2}\leqslant 2+N^{-2/3+2{\mathfrak{a}}}
.

We take
z=2+\kappa+\mathrm{i}\eta
, with
N^{-2/3+2{\mathfrak{a}}}\leqslant\kappa\leqslant{\mathfrak{a}}
and
\eta=N^{{\mathfrak{a}}}/(N\sqrt{\kappa})\ll\kappa
. With this choice, one can check that
N\eta\sqrt{\kappa}=N^{\mathfrak{a}}
and
z\in{\mathbf{D}}
, and
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m_{d}(z)]\asymp\eta/\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}\ll 1/{N\eta}
.
In this regime,
Theorem

1.2
implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|m(z)-m_{d}(z)\right|&\leqslant N^{3{\mathfrak{c}}}\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{\eta(\kappa+\eta)}}+\frac{1}{(N\eta)^{2}\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}\right)\lesssim\frac{N^{3{\mathfrak{c}}}}{N^{2{\mathfrak{a}}}(N\eta)}\ll\frac{1}{N\eta}.\end{split}

with probability
1-\OO(N^{-{\mathfrak{C}}})
with respect to the uniform measure on
\overline{\Omega}
, provided
{\mathfrak{a}}\geqslant 4{\mathfrak{c}}
. Thus it follows that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m(z)]\leqslant|m(z)+m_{d}(z)|+\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m_{d}(z)]\ll\frac{1}{N\eta}.

If there is an eigenvalue on the interval
\lambda_{i}\in[2+\kappa-\eta,2+\kappa+\eta]
, then

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m(z)]=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\left[\frac{1}{\lambda_{i}-z}\right]\geqslant\frac{1}{N}\frac{\eta}{|\lambda_{i}-(2+\kappa)|^{2}+\eta^{2}}\geqslant\frac{1}{2N\eta},

which is impossible. It follows that with probability
1-\OO(N^{-{\mathfrak{C}}})
with respect to the uniform measure on
\overline{\Omega}
,
\lambda_{2}\leqslant 2+N^{-2/3+2{\mathfrak{a}}}
.
∎

4
Expansions of Green’s Function Differences

In this section, we gather estimates on the difference in Green’s functions before and after local resampling. For Green’s functions related to the center of the local resampling, we employ Schur complement formulas, with results detailed in
Section

4.1
. This methodology has been previously utilized in similar contexts
[
40
,
12
]
to establish the local law of random
d
-regular graphs.

For Green’s function terms away from the center of the local resampling, we develop a novel expansion using the Woodbury identity, as stated in
Lemma

4.3
. This expansion represents a reorganization of the resolvent identity. In prior research
[
39
]
, resolvent identities played a pivotal role in analyzing the changes induced by simple switching in Green’s functions, yielding an expansion where the terms exhibit exponential decay in
1/\sqrt{d}
. This decay rate proves adequate when
d
scales with the size of the graph; however, in our scenario, where
d
remains fixed, the decay is too slow. Notably, in the new expansion introduced in
Lemma

4.3
, the terms decay exponentially at a rate of
1/N^{\oo(1)}
.

4.1
Switching using
the Schur complement

In this section we will use the Schur complement formula to study the Green’s function after local resampling.
We recall the local resampling and related notations from
Section

2.2
, and the spectral domain
{\mathbf{D}}
from (
2.1
).

Lemma 4.1
.

Fix
z\in{\mathbf{D}}
, a
d
-regular graph
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z)
, and an edge
(i,o)\in{\mathcal{G}}
.
We denote
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}(o,{\mathcal{G}})
,
T_{\bf S}
the local resampling with resampling data
{\bf S}=\{(l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha}),(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
around
o
, and
\widetilde{G}=T_{\bf S}G
. Condition on that
{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega(z)
and
I(\{(i,o)\}\cup\{(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]},{\mathcal{G}})=1
(recall from
Definition

3.4
), the following holds

1.

\widetilde{G}_{oo}^{(i)}-Y_{\ell}(Q)
can be rewritten as a weighted sum

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{oo}^{(i)}-Y_{\ell}(Q)=\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}(G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}-Q)+\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}_{2}}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}+\sum_{\bm{\alpha}}\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\bm{\alpha}}U_{\bm{\alpha}}}{(d-1)^{2\ell}}+{\mathcal{E}}.

(4.1)

2.

For
s,s^{\prime}\in\{o,i\}
,
\widetilde{G}_{ss^{\prime}}-P_{ss^{\prime}}
can be rewritten as a weighted sum,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widetilde{G}_{ss^{\prime}}-P_{ss^{\prime}}&=\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}(G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}-Q)+\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}_{2}}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\alpha\notin{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}(G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}-Q)\\
&+\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}_{3}}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}+\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}_{4}}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta\in{\mathsf{A}}^{\complement}_{i}}G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}\\
&+\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}_{5}}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}\atop\beta\in{\mathsf{A}}^{\complement}_{i}}G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}+{\mathfrak{c}}_{6}(Q-m_{\rm sc})+\sum_{\bm{\alpha}}\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\bm{\alpha}}U_{\bm{\alpha}}}{(d-1)^{2\ell}}+{\mathcal{E}}.\end{split}

where
{\mathsf{A}}_{i}:=\{\alpha\in[\![{1,\mu}]\!]:\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(i,l_{\alpha})=\ell+1\}
and
{\mathsf{A}}^{\complement}_{i}=[\![{\mu}]\!]\setminus{\mathsf{A}}_{i}
; the summation is over indices
\bm{\alpha}=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\cdots,\alpha_{2k})\in[\![{\mu}]\!]^{2k}
for some
2\leqslant k\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}
;

\displaystyle U_{\bm{\alpha}}=\widehat{U}_{{\alpha_{1}}{\alpha_{2}}}\widehat{U}_{{\alpha_{3}}{\alpha_{4}}}\cdots\widehat{U}_{{\alpha_{2k-1}}{\alpha_{2k}}},

where
\widehat{U}_{\alpha\alpha}=(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)
or
(d-1)^{2\ell}(Q-m_{\rm sc})
; and
\widehat{U}_{\alpha\beta}=(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}
if
\alpha\neq\beta
; and the total weights are bounded by
\OO(\poly(\ell))
:

\displaystyle\sum_{j}|{\mathfrak{c}}_{j}|+\sum_{\bm{\alpha}}|{\mathfrak{c}}_{\bm{\alpha}}|=\OO(\poly(\ell)).

The error
{\mathcal{E}}
satisfies

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{E}}|\lesssim\sum_{\alpha}|\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}-G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}|+\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta}|\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}-G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}|+\frac{1}{N^{2}}.

(4.2)

Proof of Lemma
4.1
.

We denote
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}(o,{\mathcal{G}})
the radius
\ell
neighborhood of vertex
o
, and its vertex set
\mathbb{T}
.
We denote the normalized adjacency matrix of
{\mathcal{G}}^{(i)}
as
H^{(i)}
, and its restriction to
\mathbb{T}
as
H^{(i)}_{\mathbb{T}}
. Let
\widetilde{B}
be the normalized adjacency matrix of the directed edges
\{(c_{\alpha},l_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
. We also denote the Green’s function of
{\mathcal{G}}^{(\mathbb{T})}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}^{(\mathbb{T})}
as
G^{(\mathbb{T})}
and
\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}
respectively.

Thanks to the Schur complement formula, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{oo}^{(i)}=\left(\frac{1}{H_{\mathbb{T}}^{(i)}-z-\widetilde{B}^{\top}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}\widetilde{B}}\right)_{oo}.

Since
o
has a radius
{\mathfrak{R}}/2
tree neighborhood,
H^{(i)}_{\mathbb{T}}
is the normalized adjacency matrix of a truncated
(d-1)
-ary tree,

\displaystyle m_{\rm sc}=\left(\frac{1}{H_{\mathbb{T}}^{(i)}-z-\widetilde{B}^{\top}m_{\rm sc}{\mathbb{I}}\widetilde{B}}\right)_{oo},\quad P^{(i)}:=P({\mathcal{T}}^{(i)},z,m_{\rm sc})=\frac{1}{H_{\mathbb{T}}^{(i)}-z-\widetilde{B}^{\top}m_{\rm sc}{\mathbb{I}}\widetilde{B}}.

By taking the difference of the two above expressions, we have

\widetilde{G}_{oo}^{(i)}-m_{\rm sc}=\left(\frac{1}{H_{\mathbb{T}}^{(i)}-z-\widetilde{B}^{\top}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}\widetilde{B}}-\frac{1}{H_{\mathbb{T}}^{(i)}-z-\widetilde{B}^{\top}m_{\rm sc}{\mathbb{I}}\widetilde{B}}\right)_{oo}.

Then we expand using the resolvent identity. By our assumption
{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega
, and thus
|{\widetilde{G}}^{(\mathbb{T})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}-m_{\rm sc}|\leqslant N^{-{\mathfrak{c}}}
. Thus for some sufficiently large constant
{\mathfrak{b}}
, the following holds

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\left(\frac{1}{H_{\mathbb{T}}^{(i)}-z-\widetilde{B}^{\top}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}B}-\frac{1}{H_{\mathbb{T}}^{(i)}-z-\widetilde{B}^{\top}m_{\rm sc}{\mathbb{I}}B}\right)_{oo}\\
&=\left(P^{(i)}\sum_{k=1}^{\mathfrak{b}}\left(\widetilde{B}^{\top}(\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}-m_{\rm sc}\mathbb{I})\widetilde{B}P^{(i)}\right)^{k}\right)_{oo}+\OO(N^{-2})\\
&=\left(P^{(i)}\sum_{k=1}^{\mathfrak{b}}\left(\widetilde{B}^{\top}(\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}-Q\mathbb{I}+(Q-m_{\rm sc})\mathbb{I})\widetilde{B}P^{(i)}\right)^{k}\right)_{oo}+\OO(N^{-2}).\end{split}

(4.3)

Recall
Y_{\ell}(Q)
from (
2.6
). By the same argument we also have that

\displaystyle Y_{\ell}(Q)-m_{\rm sc}=\left(P^{(i)}\sum_{k=1}^{\mathfrak{b}}\left(\widetilde{B}^{\top}(Q-m_{\rm sc})\mathbb{I}\widetilde{B}P^{(i)}\right)^{k}\right)_{oo}+\OO(N^{-2}).

(4.4)

By taking the difference of (
4.3
) and (
4.4
), up to an error
\OO(N^{-2})
, we get that the difference
\widetilde{G}_{oo}^{(i)}-Y_{\ell}(Q)
is given as

\displaystyle\left(P^{(i)}\sum_{k=1}^{\mathfrak{b}}\left(\widetilde{B}^{\top}(\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}-Q\mathbb{I}+(Q-m_{\rm sc})\mathbb{I})\widetilde{B}P^{(i)}\right)^{k}\right)_{oo}-\left(P^{(i)}\sum_{k=1}^{\mathfrak{b}}\left(\widetilde{B}^{\top}(Q-m_{\rm sc})\mathbb{I}\widetilde{B}P^{(i)}\right)^{k}\right)_{oo}.

(4.5)

By (
2.3
), we see the entries of
P^{(i)}
decay exponentially, and in particular we have that if
\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}
, then
\dist_{\mathcal{T}}(i,l_{\alpha})=\ell+1
, and

\displaystyle|P^{(i)}_{ol_{\alpha}}|\lesssim(d-1)^{-\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(o,\ell)/2}=(d-1)^{-\ell/2};

otherwise if
\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}^{\complement}
, then
o,l_{\alpha}
are in different connected components of
{\mathcal{T}}^{(i)}
, and
|P^{(i)}_{ol_{\alpha}}|=0
.
Thus the
k=1
term in (
4.5
), is given by

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}(\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}-Q)+\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}_{2}}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}},

(4.6)

where the two coefficients
|{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}|,|{\mathfrak{c}}_{2}|=\OO(1)
.

We obtain the first two terms in (
4.1
), after replacing
\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(\mathbb{T})},\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(\mathbb{T})}
in (
4.6
) by
G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})},G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}
. We collect the difference in the error term
{\mathcal{E}}
(as in (
4.2
)).

For the terms
k\geqslant 2
, in general

P^{(i)}(\widetilde{B}^{\top}V\widetilde{B}P^{(i)})^{k}
is given as a sum of terms in the following form

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\cdots,\alpha_{2k}\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}P^{(i)}_{ol_{\alpha_{1}}}V_{c_{\alpha_{1}}c_{\alpha_{2}}}P^{(i)}_{l_{\alpha_{2}}l_{\alpha_{3}}}V_{c_{\alpha_{3}}c_{\alpha_{4}}}P^{(i)}_{l_{\alpha_{4}}l_{\alpha_{5}}}\cdots V_{c_{\alpha_{2k-1}}c_{\alpha_{2k}}}P^{(i)}_{l_{\alpha_{2k}}o}.

(4.7)

Recall from (
2.3
), the contribution from
P^{(i)}
terms in (
4.7
) depends only on the distances between
l_{\alpha_{2i}}
and
l_{\alpha_{2i+1}}

\displaystyle f(\bm{\alpha}):=P^{(i)}_{ol_{\alpha_{1}}}P^{(i)}_{l_{\alpha_{2}}l_{\alpha_{3}}}\cdots P^{(i)}_{l_{\alpha_{2k}}o},\quad|f(\bm{\alpha})|\lesssim(d-1)^{-\ell}\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}(d-1)^{-\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(l_{\alpha_{2j}},l_{\alpha_{2j+1}})/2}.

We can reorganize (
4.7
) in the following way according to the equivalent class defined above

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\sum_{\bm{\alpha}}f(\bm{\alpha})V_{c_{\alpha_{1}}c_{\alpha_{2}}}V_{c_{\alpha_{3}}c_{\alpha_{4}}}\cdots V_{c_{\alpha_{2k-1}}c_{\alpha_{2k}}}\\
&=\sum_{\bm{\alpha}}f(\bm{\alpha})(d-1)^{-2k\ell}((d-1)^{2\ell}V_{c_{\alpha_{1}}c_{\alpha_{2}}})((d-1)^{2\ell}V_{c_{\alpha_{3}}c_{\alpha_{4}}})\cdots((d-1)^{2\ell}V_{c_{\alpha_{2k-1}}c_{\alpha_{2k}}})\\
&=:(d-1)^{-2\ell}\sum_{\bm{\alpha}}{\mathfrak{c}}_{\bm{\alpha}}\widehat{U}_{{\alpha_{1}}{\alpha_{2}}}\widehat{U}_{{\alpha_{3}}{\alpha_{4}}}\cdots\widehat{U}_{{\alpha_{2k-1}}{\alpha_{2k}}},\end{split}

(4.8)

where
{\mathfrak{c}}_{\bm{\alpha}}=f(\bm{\alpha})(d-1)^{-2(k-1)\ell}
and
\widehat{U}_{\alpha_{2j-1}\alpha_{2j}}:=(d-1)^{2\ell}V_{c_{\alpha_{2j-1}}c_{\alpha_{2j}}}
is one of the three terms
(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)
,
(d-1)^{2\ell}(Q-m_{\rm sc})
, or
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}
.
Finally for the summation over the weights:

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\alpha}}{\mathfrak{c}}_{\bm{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{\alpha}}|f(\bm{\alpha})|(d-1)^{-2(k-1)\ell}\lesssim\sum_{\bm{\alpha}}(d-1)^{-(k+1)\ell}\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}(d-1)^{-\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(l_{\alpha_{2j}},l_{\alpha_{2j+1}})/2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2k}}(d-1)^{2\ell}\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}\sum_{\alpha_{2j},\alpha_{2j+1}}(d-1)^{-\ell}(d-1)^{-\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(l_{\alpha_{2j}},l_{\alpha_{2j+1}})/2}=\OO(\poly(\ell)).

where in the last inequality, we used that the total number of choices for
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2k}
are
\OO((d-1)^{2\ell})
; and the following estimates

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}(d-1)^{-\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(l_{\alpha},l_{\beta})/2}=\OO(\poly(\ell)),\quad\sum_{\alpha,\beta}(d-1)^{-\ell}(d-1)^{-\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(l_{\alpha},l_{\beta})/2}=\OO(\poly(\ell)),

(4.9)

which is from the simple observation that given the index
\alpha
, and the distance
r=\dist_{\mathcal{T}}(l_{\alpha},l_{\beta})
, the number of choices for
\beta
is of order
\OO((d-1)^{r/2})
. After replacing
\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(\mathbb{T})},\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(\mathbb{T})}
in (
4.6
) by
G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})},G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}
, this finishes the first statement in
Lemma

4.1
.

Now, we generalize this argument to a term of the form
\widetilde{G}_{ss^{\prime}}-P_{ss^{\prime}}
with
s,s^{\prime}\in\{o,i\}
. We do the exact same expansion as before using the Schur complement formula,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\widetilde{G}_{s_{1}s_{2}}-P_{s_{1}s_{2}}=\left(P\sum_{k=1}^{\mathfrak{b}}\left(\widetilde{B}^{\top}(\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}-m_{\rm sc}\mathbb{I})\widetilde{B}P\right)^{k}\right)_{ss^{\prime}}+\OO(\Phi).\end{split}

(4.10)

Using the above expansion, the statement follows from the same argument as the first statement.

∎

We derive a similar expansion for factors that are Green’s function entries with at most one indices in
\{o,i\}
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Fix
z\in{\mathbf{D}}
, a
d
-regular graph
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z)
, and edges
(i,o),(b,c),(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{G}}
.
We denote
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}(o,{\mathcal{G}})
,
T_{\bf S}
the local resampling with resampling data
{\bf S}=\{(l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha}),(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
around
o
, and
\widetilde{G}=T_{\bf S}G
. Condition on that
{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega(z)
and
I(\{(i,o),(b,c),(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})\}\cup\{(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]},{\mathcal{G}})=1
(recall from
Definition

3.4
), the following holds

1.

\widetilde{G}^{(ib)}_{oc}
can be rewritten as a weighted sum

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}^{(ib)}_{oc}=\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}}{(d-1)^{\ell/2}}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}G_{c_{\alpha}c}^{(b_{\alpha}b)}+\sum_{\bm{\alpha}}{\mathfrak{c}}_{\bm{\alpha}}U_{\bm{\alpha}}+{\mathcal{E}},

where
{\mathsf{A}}_{i}:=\{\alpha\in[\![{1,\mu}]\!]:\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(i,l_{\alpha})=\ell+1\}
; the summation is over indices
\bm{\alpha}=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\cdots,\alpha_{2k})\in[\![{\mu}]\!]^{2k+1}
for some
1\leqslant k\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}
;

\displaystyle U_{\bm{\alpha}}=\widehat{U}_{{\alpha_{1}}{\alpha_{2}}}\widehat{U}_{{\alpha_{3}}{\alpha_{4}}}\cdots\widehat{U}_{{\alpha_{2k-1}}{\alpha_{2k}}}G_{c_{\alpha_{2k+1}}c}^{(b_{\alpha_{2k+1}}b)},

where
\widehat{U}_{\alpha\alpha}=(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Y_{\ell}(Q))
or
(d-1)^{2\ell}(Q-m_{\rm sc})
; and
\widehat{U}_{\alpha\beta}=(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}
if
\alpha\neq\beta
; and the total weights are bounded by
\poly(\ell)
:

\displaystyle|{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}|+\sum_{\bm{\alpha}}|{\mathfrak{c}}_{\bm{\alpha}}|=\OO(\poly(\ell)),

and

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{E}}|\lesssim\sum_{\alpha}(|\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}-G_{c_{\alpha}c}^{(b_{\alpha}b)}|+|\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}-G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}|)+\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta}|\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}-G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}|+N^{-2}.

2.

\widetilde{G}_{cc}^{(b)}
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{cc}^{(b)}=G_{cc}^{(b)}+{\mathcal{E}}.

where

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{E}}|\lesssim|\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}_{cc}-G^{(b)}_{cc}|+(d-1)^{\ell}\sum_{\alpha}|\widetilde{G}_{cc_{\alpha}}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}|^{2}.

3.

\widetilde{G}_{cc^{\prime}}^{(bb^{\prime})}
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{cc^{\prime}}^{(bb^{\prime})}=G_{cc^{\prime}}^{(bb^{\prime})}+{\mathcal{E}}.

where

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{E}}|\lesssim|\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T}bb^{\prime})}_{cc^{\prime}}-G^{(bb^{\prime})}_{cc^{\prime}}|+(d-1)^{\ell}\sum_{\alpha}(|\widetilde{G}_{cc_{\alpha}}^{(\mathbb{T}bb^{\prime})}|^{2}+|\widetilde{G}_{c^{\prime}c_{\alpha}}^{(\mathbb{T}bb^{\prime})}|^{2}).

Proof.

The proof largely emulates the proof of Lemma
4.1
, with some slight differences. We expand
\widetilde{G}_{oc}^{(ib)}
using the off-diagonal Schur complement formula as

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}^{(ib)}_{oc}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{d-1}}\sum_{\alpha}\widetilde{G}^{(ib)}_{ol_{\alpha}}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}_{c_{\alpha}c}.

We can then replace
\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}
with
G_{c_{\alpha}c}^{(b_{\alpha}b)}
, as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widetilde{G}^{(ib)}_{oc}&=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{d-1}}\sum_{\alpha}\widetilde{G}^{(ib)}_{ol_{\alpha}}G^{(b_{\alpha}b)}_{c_{\alpha}c}+{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\\
&=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{d-1}}\sum_{\alpha}(\widetilde{G}^{(ib)}_{ol_{\alpha}}-P^{(i)}_{ol_{\alpha}})G^{(b_{\alpha}b)}_{c_{\alpha}c}+P^{(i)}_{ol_{\alpha}}G^{(b_{\alpha}b)}_{c_{\alpha}c}+{\mathcal{E}}_{1}.\end{split}

(4.11)

where the error term is

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{E}}_{1}|\lesssim\sum_{\alpha}|\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}-G_{c_{\alpha}c}^{(b_{\alpha}b)}|.

For the second term on the righthand side of (
4.11
), we recall that if
\dist_{\mathcal{T}}(i,l_{\alpha})=\ell+1
,
|P^{(i)}_{ol_{\alpha}}|\asymp(d-1)^{-\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(o,\ell)/2}=(d-1)^{-\ell/2}
;
otherwise
|P^{(i)}_{ol_{\alpha}}|=0
. Thus we can rewrite it as

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}}{(d-1)^{\ell/2}}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}G^{(b_{\alpha}b)}_{c_{\alpha}c},

with
{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}=\OO(1)
.

For the first term on the righthand side of (
4.11
), we do the same expansion as in (
4.10
) from the proof of Lemma
4.1
, writing

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}(\widetilde{G}^{(ib)}_{ol_{\alpha}}-P^{(i)}_{ol_{\alpha}})G^{(b_{\alpha}b)}_{c_{\alpha}c}=\sum_{\alpha}\left(P^{(i)}\sum_{k=1}^{\mathfrak{b}}\left(\widetilde{B}^{\top}(\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}-m_{\rm sc}\mathbb{I})\widetilde{B}P^{(i)}\right)^{k}\right)_{ol_{\alpha}}G^{(b_{\alpha}b)}_{c_{\alpha}c}+\OO(N^{-2}).

By the same argument as in the proof of
Lemma

4.1
, we can replace
\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}
and
\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}
by
G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}
and
G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}
respectively, and
the terms corresponding to
k\geqslant 1
are given as a sum

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\cdots,\alpha_{2k+1}\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}P^{(i)}_{ol_{\alpha_{1}}}V_{c_{\alpha_{1}}c_{\alpha_{2}}}P^{(i)}_{l_{\alpha_{2}}l_{\alpha_{3}}}V_{c_{\alpha_{3}}c_{\alpha_{4}}}P^{(i)}_{l_{\alpha_{4}}l_{\alpha_{5}}}\cdots V_{c_{\alpha_{2k-1}}c_{\alpha_{2k}}}P^{(i)}_{l_{\alpha_{2k}}l_{\alpha_{2k+1}}}G_{c_{\alpha_{2k+1}}c}^{(b_{\alpha_{k+1}}b)}.

(4.12)

where
V_{c_{\alpha_{2j-1}}c_{\alpha_{2j}}}
is one of the three terms
(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)
,
(Q-m_{\rm sc})
, or
G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}
.

The contribution from
P^{(i)}
terms in (
4.7
) depends only on the distances between
l_{\alpha_{2i}}
and
l_{\alpha_{2i+1}}

\displaystyle f(\bm{\alpha}):=P^{(i)}_{ol_{\alpha_{1}}}P^{(i)}_{l_{\alpha_{2}}l_{\alpha_{3}}}\cdots P^{(i)}_{l_{\alpha_{2k}}l_{\alpha_{2k+1}}},\quad|f(\bm{\alpha})|\lesssim(d-1)^{-\ell/2}\prod_{j=1}^{k}(d-1)^{-\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(l_{\alpha_{2j}},l_{\alpha_{2j+1}})/2}.

We can reorganize (
4.12
) in the following way

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\sum_{\bm{\alpha}}f(\bm{\alpha})V_{c_{\alpha_{1}}c_{\alpha_{2}}}V_{c_{\alpha_{3}}c_{\alpha_{4}}}\cdots V_{c_{\alpha_{2k-1}}c_{\alpha_{2k}}}G_{c_{\alpha_{2k+1}}c}^{(b_{\alpha_{k+1}}b)}\\
&=\sum_{\bm{\alpha}}f(\bm{\alpha})(d-1)^{-2k\ell}((d-1)^{2\ell}V_{c_{\alpha_{1}}c_{\alpha_{2}}})((d-1)^{2\ell}V_{c_{\alpha_{3}}c_{\alpha_{4}}})\cdots((d-1)^{2\ell}V_{c_{\alpha_{2k-1}}c_{\alpha_{2k}}})G_{c_{\alpha_{2k+1}}c}^{(b_{\alpha_{k+1}}b)}\\
&=:\sum_{\bm{\alpha}}{\mathfrak{c}}_{\bm{\alpha}}\widehat{U}_{{\alpha_{1}}{\alpha_{2}}}\widehat{U}_{{\alpha_{3}}{\alpha_{4}}}\cdots\widehat{U}_{{\alpha_{2k-1}}{\alpha_{2k}}}G_{c_{\alpha_{2k+1}}c}^{(b_{\alpha_{k+1}}b)},\end{split}

where
{\mathfrak{c}}_{\bm{\alpha}}=f(\bm{\alpha})(d-1)^{-2k\ell}
and
\widehat{U}_{\alpha_{2j-1}\alpha_{2j}}:=(d-1)^{2\ell}V_{c_{\alpha_{2j-1}}c_{\alpha_{2j}}}
is one of the three terms
(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)
,
(Q-m_{\rm sc})
, or
G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}
.
Finally for the summation over the weights:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{\bm{\alpha}}{\mathfrak{c}}_{\bm{\alpha}}&=\sum_{\bm{\alpha}}|f(\bm{\alpha})|(d-1)^{-2k\ell}\lesssim\sum_{\bm{\alpha}}(d-1)^{-(k+1)\ell}\prod_{j=1}^{k}(d-1)^{-\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(l_{\alpha_{2j}},l_{\alpha_{2j+1}})/2}\\
&=\sum_{\alpha_{1}}(d-1)^{-\ell}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{\alpha_{2j},\alpha_{2j+1}}(d-1)^{-\ell}(d-1)^{-\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(l_{\alpha_{2j}},l_{\alpha_{2j+1}})/2}=\OO(\poly(\ell)).\end{split}

where in the last inequality, we used that the total number of choices for
\alpha_{1}
is
\OO((d-1)^{\ell})
and (
4.9
). This finishes the first statement in
Lemma

4.2
, and the error term
{\mathcal{E}}
is given by

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{E}}|\lesssim\sum_{\alpha}(|\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}-G_{c_{\alpha}c}^{(b_{\alpha}b)}|+|\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}-G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}|)+\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta}|\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}-G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}|+N^{-2}.

For the second statement, we expand
\widetilde{G}_{cc}^{(b)}
using the Schur complement formula as

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}^{(b)}_{cc}=\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}_{cc}+\frac{1}{d-1}\sum_{\alpha,\beta}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}_{cc_{\alpha}}\widetilde{G}^{(b)}_{l_{\alpha}l_{\beta}}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}_{c_{\beta}c}=\widetilde{G}^{(b)}_{cc}+{\mathcal{E}},

where

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{E}}|\lesssim|\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}_{cc}-G^{(b)}_{cc}|+(d-1)^{\ell}\sum_{\alpha}|\widetilde{G}_{cc_{\alpha}}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}|^{2}.

For the last statement, we expand
\widetilde{G}_{cc^{\prime}}^{(bb^{\prime})}
using the Schur complement formula as

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}^{(bb^{\prime})}_{cc^{\prime}}=\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T}bb^{\prime})}_{cc^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{d-1}\sum_{\alpha,\beta}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T}bb^{\prime})}_{cc_{\alpha}}\widetilde{G}^{(bb^{\prime})}_{l_{\alpha}l_{\beta}}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T}bb^{\prime})}_{c_{\beta}c^{\prime}}=G^{(bb^{\prime})}_{cc^{\prime}}+{\mathcal{E}},

where

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{E}}|\lesssim|\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T}bb^{\prime})}_{cc^{\prime}}-G^{(bb^{\prime})}_{cc^{\prime}}|+(d-1)^{\ell}\sum_{\alpha}(|\widetilde{G}_{cc_{\alpha}}^{(\mathbb{T}bb^{\prime})}|^{2}+|\widetilde{G}_{c^{\prime}c_{\alpha}}^{(\mathbb{T}bb^{\prime})}|^{2}).

∎

4.2
An Expansion using the Woodbury formula

In this section, we propose a novel expansion using the Woodbury formula. This is an important part of our analysis.
For a graph
{\mathcal{G}}
with Green’s function
G
, we consider the Green’s function after the switch
\widetilde{G}:=T_{\bf S}(G)
around some vertex
o\in[\![{N}]\!]
, and denote its normalized adjacency matrix as
\widetilde{H}
.

We compare the normalized adjacency matrix of the switched graph to that of the original graph,
\widetilde{H}-H
. We denote the rank of this difference as
r
, and rewrite
\widetilde{H}-H=UCU^{\top}
, where
U
is an
N\times r
matrix, and
C
is
r\times r
. Then, the Woodbury formula gives us

\displaystyle{\widetilde{G}}-G=(H-z+UCU^{\top})^{-1}-(H-z)^{-1}=-GU(C^{-1}+U^{\top}GU)^{-1}U^{\top}G.

(4.13)

We denote
{\mathcal{F}}
the set of edges involved in the local resampling, and
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
the graph after local resampling

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}:={\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}(o,{\mathcal{G}})\cup\bigcup_{\alpha=1}^{\mu}\{(l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha}),(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\},\quad\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}:={\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}(o,{\mathcal{G}})\cup\bigcup_{\alpha=1}^{\mu}\{(l_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}),(a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha})\}.

We will analyze this using the matrix
P:=P({\mathcal{F}},z,m_{sc})
, as was define in Definition
2.3
. Along with comparing
{\widetilde{G}}-G
. we can similarly compare
{\widetilde{P}}-P
, where
{\widetilde{P}}:=P(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},z,m_{sc})
.

Notice that
\widetilde{P}^{-1}-P^{-1}=\widetilde{H}-H
when restricted to the vertex set
\mathbb{F}
of
{\mathcal{F}}
, and it is given by
UCU^{\top}
. We can use the Woodbury formula on
P
as well, giving

\displaystyle{\widetilde{P}}-P=-PU(C^{-1}+U^{\top}PU)^{-1}U^{\top}P.

(4.14)

Our next lemma attempts to expand
{\widetilde{G}}-G
in terms of
{\widetilde{P}}-P
. We denote the adjacency matrices of our switching as

\displaystyle\xi_{\alpha}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d-1}}\left(\Delta_{l_{\alpha}a_{\alpha}}+\Delta_{b_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}-\Delta_{l_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}-\Delta_{a_{\alpha}b_{\alpha}}\right).

Then

\displaystyle UCU^{\top}=\widetilde{H}-H=\widetilde{P}^{-1}-P^{-1}=\sum_{\alpha}\xi_{\alpha}.

(4.15)

We also introduce the following matrix
F
, which is nonzero on the vertex set
\mathbb{F}
,

\displaystyle F:=\sum_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}\xi_{\alpha}+\sum_{\alpha,\beta\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}\xi_{\alpha}{\widetilde{P}}\xi_{\beta}.

Lemma 4.3
.

Fix
z\in{\mathbf{D}}
, a
d
-regular graph
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z)
, and edges
(i,o)\in{\mathcal{G}}
.
We denote
T_{\bf S}
the local resampling with resampling data
{\bf S}=\{(l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha}),(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
around
o
, and
\widetilde{G}=T_{\bf S}G
. Conditioned on that
{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega(z)
and
I(\{(i,o),(b,c),(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})\}\cup\{(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]},{\mathcal{G}})=1
(recall from
Definition

3.4
), the following holds

\displaystyle{\widetilde{G}}-G=\sum_{k\geqslant 0}GF((G|_{\mathbb{F}}-P)F)^{k}G.

Proof.

The nonzero rows of
U
are parametrized by
\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
.
By rearranging the above expression (
4.14
), we get

\displaystyle P^{-1}{\widetilde{P}}P^{-1}-P^{-1}=-U(C^{-1}+U^{\top}PU)^{-1}U^{\top}.

(4.16)

We recall that
\widetilde{P}^{-1}-P^{-1}=\widetilde{H}-H=UCU^{\top}
.
We can reorganize (
4.16
) as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}P^{-1}{\widetilde{P}}P^{-1}-P^{-1}=P^{-1}{\widetilde{P}}{\widetilde{P}}^{-1}+P^{-1}{\widetilde{P}}(P^{-1}-{\widetilde{P}}^{-1})-P^{-1}\\
&=P^{-1}{\widetilde{P}}(P^{-1}-{\widetilde{P}}^{-1})=(P^{-1}-{\widetilde{P}}^{-1}){\widetilde{P}}(P^{-1}-{\widetilde{P}}^{-1})+{\widetilde{P}}^{-1}{\widetilde{P}}(P^{-1}-{\widetilde{P}}^{-1})\\
&=(P^{-1}-{\widetilde{P}}^{-1})+(P^{-1}-{\widetilde{P}}^{-1}){\widetilde{P}}(P^{-1}-{\widetilde{P}}^{-1})\\
&=\sum_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}\xi_{\alpha}+\sum_{\alpha,\beta\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}\xi_{\alpha}{\widetilde{P}}\xi_{\beta}=F,\end{split}

(4.17)

where in the last line we used (
4.15
).

By plugging (
4.16
) and (
4.17
) into (
4.13
), and use the resolvent identity, we conclude that

\displaystyle{\widetilde{G}}-G

\displaystyle=-GU(C^{-1}+U^{\top}GU)^{-1}U^{\top}G=-GU(C^{-1}+U^{\top}PU+U^{\top}(G-P)U)^{-1}U^{\top}G

\displaystyle=-GU\left((C^{-1}+U^{\top}PU)^{-1}\sum_{k\geqslant 0}(-1)^{k}(U^{\top}(G-P)U(C^{-1}+U^{\top}PU)^{-1})^{k}\right)U^{\top}G

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geqslant 0}(-1)^{k+1}GU(C^{-1}+U^{\top}PU)^{-1}(U^{\top}(G-P)U(C^{-1}+U^{\top}PU)^{-1})^{k}U^{\top}G

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geqslant 0}GF((G|_{\mathbb{F}}-P)F)^{k}G.

∎

4.3
Switching using
the Woodbury Identity

In this section we will use the expansion formula
Lemma

4.3
to study the Green’s function after local resampling. If vertex
o
has a tree neighborhood, the following lemma gives a simple formula for the average of the Green’s function over the radius
\ell
ball. It will be used later to simplify the expression.

Lemma 4.4
.

Fix
z\in{\mathbf{D}}
, a
d
-regular graph
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z)
, and edges
(i,o)\in{\mathcal{G}}
.
We denote
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}(o,{\mathcal{G}})
,
T_{\bf S}
the local resampling with resampling data
{\bf S}=\{(l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha}),(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
around
o
, and
\widetilde{G}=T_{\bf S}G
. We denote
{\mathsf{A}}_{i}:=\{\alpha\in[\![{1,\mu}]\!]:\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(i,l_{\alpha})=\ell+1\}
and
{\mathsf{A}}_{i}^{\complement}=[\![{\mu}]\!]\setminus{\mathsf{A}}_{i}
. Condition on that
I(\{(i,o),{\mathcal{G}})=1
(recall from
Definition

3.4
), then for
{\mathsf{L}}\in\{l,a\}
and any vertex
v\in[\![{1,N}]\!]
such that
\dist_{\mathcal{G}}(v,o)\geqslant{\mathfrak{R}}/2
,

\displaystyle(d-1)^{-\ell/2}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{\mu}G_{v{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha}}=\mathfrak{t}_{1}({\mathsf{L}})G_{vo},

(4.18)

and

\displaystyle(d-1)^{-\ell/2}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}G_{v{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha}}=\mathfrak{t}_{2}({\mathsf{L}})G_{vi}+\mathfrak{t}_{3}({\mathsf{L}})G_{vo}.

(4.19)

Proof of Lemma
4.4
.

We will only prove (
4.19
), and the proof for (
4.18
) follows from essentially the same argument.

By our assumption that
I(\{(i,o)\},{\mathcal{G}})=1
, vertex
o
has radius
{\mathfrak{R}}/2
tree neighborhood. We denote
{\mathcal{S}}_{0}=\{o\}
, and
{\mathcal{S}}_{k}=\{y\in[\![{N}]\!]:\dist_{\mathcal{T}}(y,o)=k,\dist_{\mathcal{T}}(y,i)=k+1\}
for
0\leqslant k\leqslant\ell
.
{\mathcal{S}}_{k}
is the collection of vertices in
{\mathcal{T}}^{(i)}
, which are distance
k
to the root vertex
o
.
By using the equation
(G(H-z))_{vo}=\delta_{vo}=0
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{d-1}}\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}G_{vy}=zG_{vo}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{d-1}}G_{vi}.

More generally, by summing over
(G(H-z))_{vy}=0
for
y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{k}
we get

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{k}}(G(H-z))_{vy}=-z\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{k}}G_{vy}+\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{k}}(GH)_{vy}

\displaystyle=-z\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{k}}G_{vy}+\sqrt{d-1}\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{k-1}}G_{vy}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d-1}}\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{k+1}}G_{vy}.

We can convert this into an equation of the sum
\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{k}}G_{vy}
, for
k\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{k+1}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d-1}}\right)^{k+1}G_{vy}=z\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{k}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d-1}}\right)^{k}G_{vy}-\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{k-1}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d-1}}\right)^{k-1}G_{vy}.

The recursive equation gives

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}(d-1)^{-\ell/2}\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\ell}}G_{vy}\\
(d-1)^{-(\ell+1)/2}\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\ell+1}}G_{vy}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&1\\
-1&z\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}(d-1)^{-(\ell-1)/2}\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\ell-1}}G_{vy}\\
(d-1)^{-\ell/2}\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\ell}}G_{vy}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&1\\
-1&z\end{array}\right)^{\ell+1}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}G_{vi}/\sqrt{d-1}\\
G_{vo}\end{array}\right).

The eigenvalues of the transfer matrix are given by
m_{\rm sc}
and
1/m_{\rm sc}
. Therefore
(d-1)^{-\ell/2}\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\ell}}G_{vy}
,

(d-1)^{-(\ell+1)/2}\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\ell+1}}G_{vy}
can be written as linear combination of
G_{vi},G_{vo}
, with bounded coefficients, using that
|m_{\rm sc}|^{\ell}\asymp 1
.
The claim (
4.19
) follows by noticing that

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}(d-1)^{-\ell/2}G_{vl_{\alpha}}=(d-1)^{-(\ell-2)/2}\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\ell}}G_{vy}.

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}(d-1)^{-\ell/2}G_{va_{\alpha}}=(d-1)^{-\ell/2}\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\ell+1}}G_{vy}.

∎

In the following lemma, we gather estimates on the difference in Green’s functions before and after local resampling, using
Lemma

4.3
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Fix
z\in{\mathbf{D}}
, a
d
-regular graph
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z)
, and edges
(i,o),(b,c),(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{G}}
.
We denote
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}(o,{\mathcal{G}})
,
T_{\bf S}
the local resampling with resampling data
{\bf S}=\{(l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha}),(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
around
o
, and
\widetilde{G}=T_{\bf S}G
. Condition on that
{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega(z)
and
I(\{(i,o)\}\cup\{(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]},{\mathcal{G}})=1
(recall from
Definition

3.4
), the following holds

1.

Fix any vertices
s,w
in
{\mathcal{G}}
,

\widetilde{G}_{sw}-G_{sw}
can be rewritten as a weighted sum

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{sw}-G_{sw}=\sum_{{\bm{s}}}{\mathfrak{c}}_{\bm{s}}U_{{\bm{s}}}+{\mathcal{E}},

the summation is over indices
{\bm{s}}=(s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{2k})\in(\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]})^{2k}
for some
1\leqslant k\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}
;

\displaystyle U_{{\bm{s}}}=(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{ss_{1}}\widehat{U}_{s_{2}s_{3}}\widehat{U}_{s_{4}s_{5}}\cdots\widehat{U}_{s_{2k-2}s_{2k-1}}G_{s_{2k}w},

where
\widehat{U}_{s_{2j}s_{2j+1}}=(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{s_{2j}s_{2j+1}}-P_{s_{2j}s_{2j-1}})
; and the total weights are bounded by
\poly(\ell)
:

\displaystyle\sum_{{\bm{s}}}|{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\bm{s}}}|=\OO(\poly(\ell)).

2.

For
s\in\{o,i\}
and any vertex
w
in
{\mathcal{G}}
,

\widetilde{G}_{sw}-G_{sw}
can be rewritten as a weighted sum

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widetilde{G}_{sw}-G_{sw}&={\mathfrak{c}}_{1}G_{ow}+{\mathfrak{c}}_{2}G_{iw}+\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}_{3}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}G_{b_{\alpha}w}+{\mathfrak{c}}_{4}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}G_{c_{\alpha}w}}{(d-1)^{\ell/2}}+\sum_{{\bm{s}}_{1}}{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\bm{s}}_{1}}U_{{\bm{s}}_{1}}\\
&+\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}_{5}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}^{\complement}_{i}}G_{b_{\alpha}w}+{\mathfrak{c}}_{6}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}^{\complement}_{i}}G_{c_{\alpha}w}}{(d-1)^{\ell/2}}+\sum_{{\bm{s}}_{2}}{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\bm{s}}_{2}}U_{{\bm{s}}_{2}}+{\mathcal{E}},\end{split}

(4.20)

where
{\mathsf{A}}_{i}:=\{\alpha\in[\![{1,\mu}]\!]:\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(i,l_{\alpha})=\ell+1\}
and
{\mathsf{A}}_{i}^{\complement}:=[\![{\mu}]\!]\setminus{\mathsf{A}}_{i}
. The summation in the first line is over indices
{\bm{s}}_{1}=(s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{2k})\in(\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]})^{2k}
for some
1\leqslant k\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}
;

\displaystyle U_{{\bm{s}}_{1}}=\widehat{U}_{ss_{1}}\widehat{U}_{s_{2}s_{3}}\widehat{U}_{s_{4}s_{5}}\cdots\widehat{U}_{s_{2k-2}s_{2k-1}}G_{s_{2k}w},

where
\widehat{U}_{ss_{1}}=(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{ss_{1}}-P_{ss_{1}})
and
\widehat{U}_{s_{2j}s_{2j+1}}=(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{s_{2j}s_{2j+1}}-P_{s_{2j}s_{2j-1}})
; the summation in the second line is over indices
{\bm{s}}_{2}=(s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{2k+1})\in(\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]})^{2k+1}
for some
1\leqslant k\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}
;

\displaystyle U_{{\bm{s}}_{2}}=\widehat{U}_{s_{1}s_{2}}\widehat{U}_{s_{3}s_{4}}\cdots\widehat{U}_{s_{2k-1}s_{2k}}G_{s_{2k+1}w},

where
\widehat{U}_{s_{2j-1}s_{2j}}=(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{s_{2j-1}s_{2j}}-P_{s_{2j-1}s_{2j}})
;
and the total weights are bounded by
\poly(\ell)
:

\displaystyle|{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}|+|{\mathfrak{c}}_{2}|+|{\mathfrak{c}}_{3}|+|{\mathfrak{c}}_{4}|+|{\mathfrak{c}}_{5}|+|{\mathfrak{c}}_{6}|+\sum_{{\bm{s}}_{1}}|{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\bm{s}}_{1}}|+\sum_{{\bm{s}}_{2}}|{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\bm{s}}_{2}}|=\OO(\poly(\ell)).

3.

For any vertex
w
in
{\mathcal{G}}
,
(1/\widetilde{G}_{ww})-(1/G_{ww})
can be rewritten as a weighted sum

\displaystyle(1/\widetilde{G}_{ww})-(1/G_{ww})=\sum_{1\leqslant j\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}}\frac{1}{G_{ww}^{j+1}}\sum_{{\bm{s}}_{j}}{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\bm{s}}_{j}}U_{{\bm{s}}_{j}}+{\mathcal{E}},

where for the
j
-th term, the summation is over indices

{\bm{s}}_{j}=(s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{2k})\in(\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]})^{2k}
for some
j\leqslant k\leqslant j{\mathfrak{b}}
;

\displaystyle U_{{\bm{s}}_{j}}=\prod_{1\leqslant r\leqslant j}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{s_{2r-1}w}G_{s_{2r}w}\prod_{j+1\leqslant r\leqslant k}\widehat{U}_{s_{2r-1}s_{2r}},

where
\widehat{U}_{s_{2j}s_{2j+1}}=(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{s_{2j}s_{2j+1}}-P_{s_{2j}s_{2j-1}})
; and the total weights are bounded by
\OO(\poly(\ell))
.

In all cases, the error
{\mathcal{E}}
satisfies

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}\leqslant N^{-2}.

Proof of Lemma
4.5
.

We first prove the second term on the righthand side of (
4.23
). The first statement is easier, and can be proven in the same way.
We recall the notations from
Lemma

4.3
, in particular
P:=P({\mathcal{T}},z,m_{sc})
. Thus, by Lemma
4.3
, there is some finite
{\mathfrak{b}}
such that,

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}-G=\sum_{k=1}^{\mathfrak{b}}GF\left((G-P)F\right)^{k-1}G+\OO(N^{-2}),

(4.21)

where

\displaystyle F:=\sum_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}\xi_{\alpha}+\sum_{\alpha,\beta\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}\xi_{\alpha}{\widetilde{P}}\xi_{\beta},

which has nonzero entries only on the vertices
\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
, and for any
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in[\![{\mu}]\!]

\displaystyle|F_{{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha}{\mathsf{L}}^{\prime}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}|\lesssim(d-1)^{-\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(l_{\alpha},l_{\alpha^{\prime}})},\quad{\mathsf{L}},{\mathsf{L}}^{\prime}\in\{l,a,b,c\}.

(4.22)

Thus we can rewrite the
k
-th term in (
4.21
) as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\left(GF((G-P)F)^{k-1}G\right)_{sw}\\
&=\sum_{{\bm{s}}}(G_{ss_{1}}-P_{ss_{1}})F_{s_{1}s_{2}}(G_{s_{2}s_{3}}-P_{s_{2}s_{3}})F_{s_{3}s_{4}}\cdots(G_{s_{2k-2}s_{2k-1}}-P_{s_{2k-2}s_{2k-1}})F_{s_{2k-1}s_{2k}}G_{s_{2k}w}\\
&+\sum_{{\bm{s}}}P_{ss_{1}}F_{s_{1}s_{2}}(G_{s_{2}s_{3}}-P_{s_{2}s_{3}})F_{s_{3}s_{4}}\cdots(G_{s_{2k-2}s_{2k-1}}-P_{s_{2k-2}s_{2k-1}})F_{s_{2k-1}s_{2k}}G_{s_{2k}w},\end{split}

(4.23)

where the summation is over
{\bm{s}}=(s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{2k})\in(\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]})^{2k}
.
Roughly speaking, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widetilde{G}-G&=GFG+\text{higher order terms}\\
&=PFG+(G-P)FG+\text{higher order terms}\end{split}

The terms linear in
G
on the righthand side of (
4.20
) corresponds to
PFG
.

We can reorganize the first term on the righthand side of (
4.23
) in the following way

\displaystyle\sum_{{\bm{s}}}{\mathfrak{c}}_{\bm{s}}\widehat{U}_{ss_{1}}\widehat{U}_{s_{2}s_{3}}\widehat{U}_{s_{4}s_{5}}\cdots\widehat{U}_{s_{2k-2}s_{2k-1}}G_{s_{2k}w},\quad{\mathfrak{c}}_{\bm{s}}:=f({\bm{s}})(d-1)^{-2k\ell},

(4.24)

where
\widehat{U}_{ss_{1}}=(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{ss_{1}}-P_{ss_{1}})
and
\widehat{U}_{s_{2j}s_{2j+1}}=(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{s_{2j}s_{2j+1}}-P_{s_{2j}s_{2j-1}})
,
and for
\bm{s}=({\mathsf{L}}^{(1)}_{\alpha_{1}},{\mathsf{L}}^{(2)}_{\alpha_{2}},\cdots,{\mathsf{L}}^{(2k)}_{\alpha_{2k}})
and
{\mathsf{L}}^{(1)},{\mathsf{L}}^{(2)},\cdots,{\mathsf{L}}^{(2k)}\in\{l,a,b,c\}
,

\displaystyle f({\bm{s}})=F_{s_{1}s_{2}}F_{s_{3}s_{4}}\cdots F_{s_{2k-1}s_{2k}},\quad|f({\bm{s}})|\leqslant\prod_{j=1}^{k}(d-1)^{-\dist_{\mathcal{T}}(l_{\alpha_{2j-1}},l_{\alpha_{2j}})},

where we used (
4.22
). For the summation over the weights:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{{\bm{s}}}|{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\bm{s}}}|&=\sum_{{\bm{s}}}|f({\bm{s}})|(d-1)^{-2k\ell}\lesssim\sum_{\bm{\alpha}}4^{k}(d-1)^{-2k\ell}\prod_{j=1}^{k}(d-1)^{-\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(l_{\alpha_{2j-1}},l_{\alpha_{2j}})/2}\\
&\leqslant 4^{k}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{\alpha_{2j-1},\alpha_{2j}}(d-1)^{-\ell}(d-1)^{-\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(l_{\alpha_{2j-1}},l_{\alpha_{2j}})/2}=\OO(\poly(\ell)).\end{split}

where in the last inequality, we used (
4.9
). These terms in (
4.24
) give the summation over
{\bm{s}}_{1}
in the first line of (
4.20
).

For the second term on the righthand side of (
4.23
), with
k\geqslant 2
, we reorganize them as

\displaystyle\sum_{{\bm{s}}}{\mathfrak{c}}_{\bm{s}}\widehat{U}_{s_{2}s_{3}}\widehat{U}_{s_{4}s_{5}}\cdots\widehat{U}_{s_{2k-2}s_{2k-1}}G_{s_{2k}w},\quad{\mathfrak{c}}_{\bm{s}}=f({\bm{s}})(d-1)^{-2(k-1)\ell},

(4.25)

where
\widehat{U}_{s_{2j}s_{2j+1}}=(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{s_{2j}s_{2j+1}}-P_{s_{2j}s_{2j-1}})
,
and for
\bm{s}=({\mathsf{L}}^{(1)}_{\alpha_{1}},{\mathsf{L}}^{(2)}_{\alpha_{2}},\cdots,{\mathsf{L}}^{(2k)}_{\alpha_{2k}})
and
{\mathsf{L}}^{(1)},{\mathsf{L}}^{(2)},\cdots,{\mathsf{L}}^{(2k)}\in\{l,a,b,c\}
,

\displaystyle f({\bm{s}})=P_{ss_{1}}F_{s_{1}s_{2}}F_{s_{3}s_{4}}\cdots F_{s_{2k-1}s_{2k}},\quad|f({\bm{s}})|\leqslant(d-1)^{-\ell/2}\prod_{j=1}^{k}(d-1)^{-\dist_{\mathcal{T}}(l_{\alpha_{2j-1}},l_{\alpha_{2j}})},

where we used (
4.22
). The summation over the weights:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{{\bm{s}}}|{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\bm{s}}}|&=\sum_{{\bm{s}}}|f({\bm{s}})|(d-1)^{-2(k-1)\ell}\lesssim\sum_{\bm{\alpha}}4^{k}(d-1)^{-k\ell}\prod_{j=1}^{k}(d-1)^{-\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(l_{\alpha_{2j-1}},l_{\alpha_{2j}})/2}\\
&=4^{k}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{\alpha_{2j-1},\alpha_{2j}}(d-1)^{-\ell}(d-1)^{-\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(l_{\alpha_{2j-1}},l_{\alpha_{2j}})/2}=\OO(\poly(\ell)).\end{split}

where in the last inequality, we used (
4.9
).
These terms in (
4.25
) give the summation over
{\bm{s}}_{2}
in the first line of (
4.20
).

For the second term on the righthand side of (
4.23
), with
k=1
(i.e. the term
(PFG)_{sw}
),
we can sum over
s_{1}
first. Since
s\in\{o,i\}
,
P_{ss_{1}}\neq 0
only if
s_{1}\in\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
. Therefore fix
s_{2}={\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha_{2}}
for some
{\mathsf{L}}\in\{l,a,b,c\}
and
1\leqslant\alpha_{2}\leqslant\mu
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{1}}P_{ss_{1}}F_{s_{1}s_{2}}=\sum_{{\alpha_{1}}\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}\left(P_{sl_{\alpha_{1}}}F_{l_{\alpha_{1}}{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha_{2}}}+P_{sa_{\alpha_{1}}}F_{a_{\alpha_{1}}{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha_{2}}}\right)

(4.26)

Recall the set
{\mathsf{A}}_{i}=\{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]:\dist_{\mathcal{T}}(i,l_{\alpha})=\ell+1\}
. First we notice that the above summation depends only on if
\alpha_{2}\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}
or
\alpha_{2}\notin{\mathsf{A}}_{i}
. Moreover, thanks to (
4.22
) and (
4.9
).

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{s_{1}}P_{ss_{1}}F_{s_{1}s_{2}}\right|\lesssim\sum_{\alpha}(d-1)^{-\ell/2}(d-1)^{-\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(l_{\alpha},l_{\alpha_{2}})}\lesssim(d-1)^{-\ell/2}.

From the discussion above we conclude that (
4.26
) is in the following form

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{1}}P_{ss_{1}}F_{s_{1}s_{2}}=(d-1)^{-\ell/2}(\widehat{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}({\mathsf{L}})\bm{1}(\alpha_{2}\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i})+\widehat{\mathfrak{c}}_{2}({\mathsf{L}})\bm{1}(\alpha_{2}\not\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i})),

where the two coefficients
|\widehat{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}({\mathsf{L}})|,|\widehat{\mathfrak{c}}_{2}({\mathsf{L}})|\leqslant\poly(\ell)
. Separating
{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha_{2}}
in (
4.26
) into two cases:
{\mathsf{L}}\in\{a,l\}
or
{\mathsf{L}}\in\{b,c\}
, and using Lemma
4.4
for the case
{\mathsf{L}}\in\{a,l\}
, we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\sum_{s_{1},s_{2}}P_{ss_{1}}F_{s_{1}s_{2}}G_{s_{2}w}=\sum_{s_{2}={\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha_{2}}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}({\mathsf{L}})\bm{1}(\alpha_{2}\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i})+\widehat{\mathfrak{c}}_{2}({\mathsf{L}})\bm{1}(\alpha_{2}\not\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}))G_{s_{2}w}\\
&={\mathfrak{c}}_{1}G_{ow}+{\mathfrak{c}}_{2}G_{iw}+\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}_{3}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}G_{b_{\alpha}w}+{\mathfrak{c}}_{4}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}G_{c_{\alpha}w}+{\mathfrak{c}}_{5}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}^{\complement}_{i}}G_{b_{\alpha}w}+{\mathfrak{c}}_{6}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}^{\complement}_{i}}G_{c_{\alpha}w}}{(d-1)^{\ell/2}},\end{split}

where the coefficients
|{\mathfrak{c}}_{j}|\leqslant\OO(\poly(\ell))
for
1\leqslant j\leqslant 6
depending on
s\in\{o,i\}
.
This finishes the proof of the second statement in
Lemma

4.5
.

For the first statement in
Lemma

4.5
, we have exactly the same expansion as in (
4.23
), and the first statement in
Lemma

4.5
follows from the same analysis as the first term on the righthand side of (
4.23
).

For the last statement, by Taylor expansion

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\widetilde{G}_{ww}}-\frac{1}{G_{ww}}=\sum_{1\leqslant j\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}}\frac{(G_{ww}-\widetilde{G}_{ww})^{j}}{G_{ww}^{j+1}}+\OO(N^{-2}).

(4.27)

Then the last statement in
Lemma

4.5
follows from the first statement.

∎

In the following lemma, we gather estimates on the difference of
Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)
before and after local resampling, using
Lemma

4.3
.

Lemma 4.6
.

Fix
z\in{\mathbf{D}}
, a
d
-regular graph
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z)
, and edges
(i,o),(b,c),(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{G}}
.
We denote
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}(o,{\mathcal{G}})
,
T_{\bf S}
the local resampling with resampling data
{\bf S}=\{(l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha}),(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
around
o
, and
\widetilde{G}=T_{\bf S}G
. Condition on that
{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega(z)
and
I(\{(i,o)\}\cup\{(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]},{\mathcal{G}})=1
(recall from
Definition

3.4
), then
(\widetilde{Q}-Y_{\ell}(\widetilde{Q}))-(Q-Y_{\ell}(Q))
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(Q)}{Nd}\sum_{u,v}A_{uv}\left(\sum_{{\bm{s}}_{1}}{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\bm{s}}_{1}}U^{(u)}_{{\bm{s}}_{1}}+\sum_{0\leqslant j\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}}\frac{G_{uv}}{G_{uu}^{j+1}}\sum_{{\bm{s}}_{2}}{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\bm{s}}_{2}}U^{(u,v)}_{{\bm{s}}_{2}}+\sum_{0\leqslant j\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}}\frac{1}{G_{uu}^{j+1}}\sum_{{\bm{s}}_{3}}{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\bm{s}}_{3}}U^{(u,v)}_{{\bm{s}}_{3}}\right)+{\mathcal{E}},\end{split}

(4.28)

where
the first summation is over indices
{\bm{s}}_{1}=(s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{2k})\in(\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]})^{2k}
for some
1\leqslant k\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}
;

\displaystyle U^{(u,v)}_{{\bm{s}}_{1}}=(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{vs_{1}}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{s_{2}v}\widehat{U}_{s_{3}s_{4}}\widehat{U}_{s_{5}s_{6}}\cdots\widehat{U}_{s_{2k-1}s_{2k}};

the second summation is over indices
{\bm{s}}_{2}=(s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{2k})\in(\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]})^{2k}
for some
j+1\leqslant k\leqslant(j+1){\mathfrak{b}}
;

\displaystyle U^{(u,v)}_{{\bm{s}}_{2}}=(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{us_{1}}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{s_{2}v}\prod_{2\leqslant r\leqslant j+1}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{s_{2r-1}u}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{s_{2r}u}\prod_{j+2\leqslant r^{\prime}\leqslant k}\widehat{U}_{s_{2r^{\prime}-1}s_{2r^{\prime}}};

the third summation is over indices
{\bm{s}}_{3}=(s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{2k})\in(\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]})^{2k}
for some
j+2\leqslant k\leqslant(j+2){\mathfrak{b}}
;

\displaystyle U^{(u,v)}_{{\bm{s}}_{3}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{1\leqslant r\leqslant 2}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{us_{2r-1}}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{s_{2r}v}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{3\leqslant r^{\prime}\leqslant j+2}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{s_{2r^{\prime}-1}u}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{s_{2r^{\prime}}u}\prod_{j+3\leqslant r^{\prime\prime}\leqslant k}\widehat{U}_{s_{2r^{\prime\prime}-1}s_{2r^{\prime\prime}}}.

In all the above cases
\widehat{U}_{s_{2k-1}s_{2k}}=(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{s_{2k-1}s_{2k}}-P_{s_{2k-1}s_{2k}})
; the total summation of weights
|{\mathfrak{c}}_{\bm{s}}|
is bounded by
\OO(\poly(\ell))
; and the error
{\mathcal{E}}
satisfies

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}\leqslant N^{-2}+\Phi^{2}.

Proof of Lemma
4.6
.

By (
2.9
),

\displaystyle(\widetilde{Q}-Y_{\ell}(\widetilde{Q}))-(Q-Y_{\ell}(Q))=(1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(Q))(\widetilde{Q}-Q)+\OO(|\widetilde{Q}-Q|^{2}).

(4.29)

Next, we investigate the difference
\widetilde{Q}-Q
.
By the Schur complement formula, we can write

\displaystyle G_{vv}^{(u)}=G_{vv}-\frac{G_{uv}^{2}}{G_{uu}}.

(4.30)

By

Lemma

4.5
, for
x,y\in\{u,v\}
,

\widetilde{G}_{xy}-G_{xy}
can be rewritten as a weighted sum

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{xy}-G_{xy}=\sum_{{\bm{s}}}{\mathfrak{c}}_{\bm{s}}U_{{\bm{s}}}+{\mathcal{E}},

(4.31)

the summation is over indices
{\bm{s}}=(s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{2k})\in(\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]})^{2k}
for some
1\leqslant k\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}
;

\displaystyle U_{{\bm{s}}}=(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{xs_{1}}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{s_{2k}y}\widehat{U}_{s_{2}s_{3}}\widehat{U}_{s_{4}s_{5}}\cdots\widehat{U}_{s_{2k-2}s_{2k-1}},

where
\widehat{U}_{s_{2j}s_{2j+1}}=(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{s_{2j}s_{2j+1}}-P_{s_{2j}s_{2j-1}})
; and the total weights are bounded by
\OO(\poly(\ell))
:

\displaystyle\sum_{{\bm{s}}}|{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\bm{s}}}|=\OO(\poly(\ell)).

For the difference
\widetilde{G}_{vv}^{(u)}-G_{vv}^{(u)}
, using (
4.30
) and (
4.27
), we can rewrite it as

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{vv}^{(u)}-G_{vv}^{(u)}

\displaystyle=(\widetilde{G}_{vv}-G_{vv})+\frac{2G_{uv}(\widetilde{G}_{uv}-G_{uv})}{G_{uu}}\sum_{0\leqslant j\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}}\left(\frac{G_{uu}-\widetilde{G}_{uu}}{G_{uu}}\right)^{j}

\displaystyle+\frac{(\widetilde{G}_{uv}-G_{uv})^{2}}{G_{uu}}\sum_{0\leqslant j\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}}\left(\frac{G_{uu}-\widetilde{G}_{uu}}{G_{uu}}\right)^{j}+\OO(N^{-2}).

Then we can replace the differences
\widetilde{G}_{vv}-G_{vv}
,
\widetilde{G}_{uu}-G_{uu}
and
\widetilde{G}_{uv}-G_{uv}
by (
4.31
), giving

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{vv}^{(u)}-G_{vv}^{(u)}=\sum_{{\bm{s}}_{1}}{\mathfrak{c}}_{\bm{s}}U^{(v)}_{{\bm{s}}_{1}}+\sum_{0\leqslant j\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}}\frac{G_{uv}}{G_{uu}^{j+1}}\sum_{{\bm{s}}_{2}}{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\bm{s}}_{2}}U^{(u,v)}_{{\bm{s}}_{2}}+\sum_{0\leqslant j\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}}\frac{1}{G_{uu}^{j+1}}\sum_{{\bm{s}}_{3}}{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\bm{s}}_{3}}U^{(u,v)}_{{\bm{s}}_{3}}+{\mathcal{E}}

(4.32)

where the first summation is over indices
{\bm{s}}_{1}=(s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{2{k_{1}}})\in(\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]})^{2{k_{1}}}
for some
1\leqslant{k_{1}}\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}
;

\displaystyle U^{(v)}_{{\bm{s}}_{1}}=(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{vs_{1}}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{s_{2}v}\widehat{U}_{s_{3}s_{4}}\widehat{U}_{s_{5}s_{6}}\cdots\widehat{U}_{s_{2{k_{1}}-1}s_{2{k_{1}}}};

the second summation is over indices
{\bm{s}}_{2}=(s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{2{k_{2}}})\in(\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]})^{2{k_{2}}}
for some
j+1\leqslant{k_{2}}\leqslant(j+1){\mathfrak{b}}
;

\displaystyle U^{(u,v)}_{{\bm{s}}_{2}}=(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{us_{1}}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{s_{2}v}\prod_{2\leqslant r\leqslant j+1}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{s_{2r-1}u}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{s_{2r}u}\prod_{j+2\leqslant r^{\prime}\leqslant{k_{2}}}\widehat{U}_{s_{2r^{\prime}-1}s_{2r^{\prime}}};

the third summation is over indices
{\bm{s}}_{3}=(s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{2{k_{3}}})\in(\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]})^{2{k_{3}}}
for some
j+2\leqslant{k_{3}}\leqslant(j+2){\mathfrak{b}}
;

\displaystyle U^{(u,v)}_{{\bm{s}}_{3}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{1\leqslant r\leqslant 2}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{us_{2r-1}}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{s_{2r}v}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{3\leqslant r^{\prime}\leqslant j+2}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{s_{2r^{\prime}-1}u}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{s_{2r^{\prime}}u}\prod_{j+3\leqslant r^{\prime\prime}\leqslant{k_{3}}}\widehat{U}_{s_{2r^{\prime\prime}-1}s_{2r^{\prime\prime}}};

In all the above cases
\widehat{U}_{s_{2k}s_{2k+1}}=(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{s_{2k}s_{2k+1}}-P_{s_{2k}s_{2k-1}})
; and the total summation of weights
|{\mathfrak{c}}_{\bm{s}}|
is bounded by
\OO(\poly(\ell))
.

If we average over the edges
(u,v)\in{\mathcal{G}}
in (
4.32
), by the Ward identity, we conclude that

\displaystyle|\widetilde{Q}-Q|\lesssim\Phi.

(4.33)

The claim of
Lemma

4.6
follows from plugging (
4.32
) and (
4.33
) into (
4.29
).

∎

5
Bounds on Error Terms

In the previous section, we collect various expansions of the differences in Green’s functions before and after local resampling. In this section, we show that the error terms in these differences are in fact small. Fix
z\in{\mathbf{D}}
, a
d
-regular graph
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z)
, we introduce the error parameter

\displaystyle\Pi_{p}(z):=\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z))\left(|Q(z;{\mathcal{G}})-Y_{\ell}(Q(z;{\mathcal{G}}))|+\Phi(z)\right)^{2p-1},

(5.1)

and recall
\Phi(z)=N^{\mathfrak{c}}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m(z)]/(N\eta)
from
Definition

3.1
.

First, we will show that if we delete a common neighbor of two vertices, the expected value of the Green’s function is as small as it would be if the two vertices were chosen at random.

Proposition 5.1
.

Fix
z\in{\mathbf{D}}
and a
d
-regular graph
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z)
. Let
\Pi_{p}(z)
be as in (
5.1
). We denote the indicator function
I(o,{\mathcal{G}})=1
if for any
v\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}(o,{\mathcal{G}})
,
v
has a radius
{\mathfrak{R}}
tree neighborhood. We denote
T_{\bf S}
the local resampling around
o
with resampling data
\bf S
, and
\widetilde{G}=T_{\bf S}G
. Then for any neighbor vertices
i,j
of
o
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})|G^{(o)}_{ij}(z)|^{2}\Pi_{p}(z)]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}(z)].

(5.2)

We need the following estimates for the proof of
Proposition

5.1
.

Lemma 5.2
.

Fix
z\in{\mathbf{D}}
and a
d
-regular graph
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z)
. We denote
T_{\bf S}
the local resampling around
o
with resampling data
\bf S
,
\mathbb{T}
the vertex set of
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}(o,{\mathcal{G}})
and the set
\mathbb{W}=\{b_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
.
Given indices
\alpha\neq\beta
, and a vertex
y\sim c_{\beta}
(where the adjacency
\sim
is with respect to the graph
{\mathcal{G}}
) or
y\in\{a_{\gamma}\}_{\gamma\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
, the following holds

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[|(G^{(\mathbb{T})}(G^{(\mathbb{T})}|_{\mathbb{W}})^{-1}G^{(\mathbb{T})})_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}|^{2}],\quad\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[|G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}|^{2}],\quad\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[|G_{c_{\alpha}y}^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}|^{2}]\lesssim\Phi+N^{-1+{\mathfrak{c}}}.

(5.3)

Proof of
Lemma

5.2
.

We only proof the first inequality in (
5.3
) as the other proofs are similar.
We recall the set of resampling data
{\sf F}({\mathcal{G}})\subset{\sf S}({\mathcal{G}})
from
Lemma

2.11
. Then

Lemma

2.10
and
Theorem

2.4
together imply that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[|(G^{(\mathbb{T})}(G^{(\mathbb{T})}|_{\mathbb{W}})^{-1}G^{(\mathbb{T})})_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}|^{2}]=\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[\bm{1}({\bf S}\in{\sf F}({\mathcal{G}}))|(G^{(\mathbb{T})}(G^{(\mathbb{T})}|_{\mathbb{W}})^{-1}G^{(\mathbb{T})})_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}|^{2}]+\OO(N^{-1+{\mathfrak{c}}}).

In the rest, we restrict to the event that
{\bf S}\in{\sf F}({\mathcal{G}})
. We denote the diagonal matrix
D=\diag(G^{(\mathbb{T})}|_{\mathbb{W}})
, then we can Taylor expand the inverse as

\displaystyle(G^{(\mathbb{T})}|_{\mathbb{W}})^{-1}=\sum_{k=0}^{\mathfrak{b}}D^{-1}\left((G^{(\mathbb{T})}|_{\mathbb{W}}-D)D^{-1}\right)^{k}+\OO(N^{-2}).

Excluding some negligible part, we can rewrite
(G^{(\mathbb{T})}(G^{(\mathbb{T})}|_{\mathbb{W}})^{-1}G^{(\mathbb{T})})_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}
as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\bigg[G^{(\mathbb{T})}\sum_{k=0}^{\mathfrak{b}}D^{-1}\left((G^{(\mathbb{T})}|_{\mathbb{W}}-D)D^{-1}\right)^{k}G^{(\mathbb{T})}\bigg]_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}\\
&=\sum_{k=0}^{\mathfrak{b}}\sum_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\cdots,\alpha_{k+1}\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}G^{(\mathbb{T})}_{c_{\alpha}b_{\alpha_{1}}}D^{-1}_{b_{\alpha_{1}}b_{\alpha_{1}}}V_{b_{\alpha_{1}}b_{\alpha_{2}}}D^{-1}_{b_{\alpha_{2}}b_{\alpha_{2}}}V_{b_{\alpha_{2}}b_{\alpha_{3}}}D^{-1}_{b_{\alpha_{3}}b_{\alpha_{3}}}\cdots D^{-1}_{b_{\alpha_{k+1}}b_{\alpha_{k+1}}}G^{(\mathbb{T})}_{b_{\alpha_{k+1}}c_{\beta}}.\end{split}

(5.4)

For each summand in (
5.4
): if
k=0
, since
\alpha\neq\beta
, either
\alpha\neq\alpha_{1}
or
\beta\neq\alpha_{1}
; if
k\geqslant 1
,
V_{b_{\alpha_{1}}b_{\alpha_{2}}}
is nonzero only if
\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}
, in which case
V_{b_{\alpha_{1}}b_{\alpha_{2}}}=G^{(\mathbb{T})}_{b_{\alpha_{1}}b_{\alpha_{2}}}
. Therefore, by Theorem
2.4
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}|V_{b_{\alpha}b_{\beta}}|\leqslant(d-1)^{\ell}\varepsilon\leqslant(d-1)^{-\ell}.

Therefore, as we know that to get from
\alpha
to
\beta
we must change indices, we can then bound the sum (
5.4
) as

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:firsterror}|\leqslant\frac{1}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\gamma\neq\alpha}|G^{(\mathbb{T})}_{c_{\alpha}b_{\gamma}}|+\frac{1}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\gamma\neq\beta}|G^{(\mathbb{T})}_{c_{\beta}b_{\gamma}}|+\frac{1}{(d-1)^{2\ell}}\sum_{\gamma\neq\gamma^{\prime}}|G^{(\mathbb{T})}_{b_{\gamma}b_{\gamma^{\prime}}}|.

Taking square and expectation over
{\bf S}
, thanks to the Ward identity, we conclude

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\bf S}}[|\eqref{eq:firsterror}|^{2}]\lesssim\sum_{\gamma\neq\alpha}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[|G^{(\mathbb{T})}_{c_{\alpha}b_{\gamma}}|^{2}]}{(d-1)^{\ell}}+\sum_{\gamma\neq\beta}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[|G^{(\mathbb{T})}_{c_{\beta}b_{\gamma}}|^{2}]}{(d-1)^{\ell}}+\sum_{\gamma\neq\gamma^{\prime}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[|G^{(\mathbb{T})}_{b_{\gamma}b_{\gamma^{\prime}}}|^{2}]}{(d-1)^{2\ell}}\lesssim\Phi.

This finishes the proof for the first statement in (
5.3
).

∎

Proof of
Proposition

5.1
.

We will do a local resampling around the vertex
o
. We split the lefthand side of (
5.2
) into two terms

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\mathbb{E}\left[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})|G^{(o)}_{ij}|^{2}\Pi_{p}\right]\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})I(o,\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\overline{\Omega})|G^{(o)}_{ij}|^{2}\Pi_{p}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[1-I(o,\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\overline{\Omega})]|G^{(o)}_{ij}|^{2}\Pi_{p}\right]\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})I(o,\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\overline{\Omega})|G^{(o)}_{ij}|^{2}\Pi_{p}\right]+\OO\left(\frac{1}{N^{1-2{\mathfrak{c}}}}\mathbb{E}\left[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})|G^{(o)}_{ij}|^{2}\Pi_{p}\right]\right)\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})I(o,\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\overline{\Omega})|G^{(o)}_{ij}|^{2}\Pi_{p}\right]+\OO\left(\frac{1}{N^{1-2{\mathfrak{c}}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\Pi_{p}\right]\right),\\
\end{split}

(5.5)

where in the third line we used
Lemma

3.5
, that
\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[1-I(o,{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}})\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\overline{\Omega})]\leqslant N^{-1+2{\mathfrak{c}}}
; in the last line we used that for
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega
,
|G_{ij}^{(o)}|\lesssim 1
.

Next we analyze the first term in the last line of (
5.5
).

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\mathbb{E}\left[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})I(o,\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\overline{\Omega})|G^{(o)}_{ij}|^{2}\Pi_{p}\right]\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})I(o,\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)|G^{(o)}_{ij}|^{2}\Pi_{p}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})I(o,\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Omega)|G^{(o)}_{ij}|^{2}\Pi_{p}\right].\end{split}

(5.6)

For the last term in (
5.6
), we have that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\mathbb{E}\left[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})I(o,\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Omega)|G^{(o)}_{ij}|^{2}\Pi_{p}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Omega)\Pi_{p}\right]\\
&\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\Pi_{p}^{2p/(2p-1)}\right]^{1-1/2p}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Omega)\right]^{1/2p}\\
&\lesssim N^{-({\mathfrak{d}}-3)/2p}\mathbb{E}\left[\Pi_{p}^{2p/(2p-1)}\right]^{1-1/2p},\end{split}

(5.7)

where in the first line we used that
|G_{ij}^{(o)}|\lesssim 1
for
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega
; in the second line we used Hölder’s inequality; in the last line we used Jensen’s inequality and
\mathbb{P}(\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Omega)\lesssim N^{-{\mathfrak{d}}+3}
, which follows from Proposition
2.4
.
Recall the expression of
\Pi_{p}
from (
5.1
), we conclude that

\displaystyle|\eqref{e:ffbbcopy}|\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}],

provided we take that
{\mathfrak{d}}\geqslant 2p+3
.

By Lemma
2.9
, we can rewrite the first term on the righthand side of (
5.6
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})I(o,\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}){\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)}|G^{(o)}_{ij}|^{2}\Pi_{p}\right]&=\mathbb{E}\left[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})I(o,\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}){\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)}|\widetilde{G}^{(o)}_{ij}|^{2}\widetilde{\Pi}_{p}\right]\\
&\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})|\widetilde{G}^{(o)}_{ij}|^{2}\Pi_{p}\right],\end{split}

(5.8)

where with overwhelmingly high probability,

\displaystyle\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\widetilde{\Pi}_{p}=\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)(|Q(z;{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}})-Y(Q(z;{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}))|+\widetilde{\Phi}(z))^{2p-1}\lesssim\Pi_{p}.

In the following we estimate the righthand side of (
5.8
). We denote
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}(o,{\mathcal{G}})
and its vertex set

\mathbb{T}
, and
P:=P({\mathcal{T}},z,m_{\rm sc})
. We notice that since
i,j
are distinct neighboring vertices of
o
,
i,j
are in different connected components of
{\mathcal{T}}^{(o)}
Thus
P^{(o)}_{ij}=0
, and
\widetilde{G}^{(o)}_{ij}=\widetilde{G}^{(o)}_{ij}-P^{(o)}_{ij}
. We will use the same argument as in the proof of
Lemma

4.1
. In the rest, we restrict to the event that
{\bf S}\in{\sf F}({\mathcal{G}})
from
Lemma

2.11
, then

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}^{(o)}_{ij}=\left(P^{(o)}\sum_{k=1}^{\mathfrak{b}}\left(\widetilde{B}^{\top}(\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}-m_{\rm sc}\mathbb{I})\widetilde{B}P^{(o)}\right)^{k}\right)_{ij}+\OO(N^{-2}).

(5.9)

For any
1\leqslant k\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}
, the above expression is a sum of terms in the following form

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\cdots,\alpha_{2k}\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}P^{(o)}_{il_{\alpha_{1}}}V_{c_{\alpha_{1}}c_{\alpha_{2}}}P^{(o)}_{l_{\alpha_{2}}l_{\alpha_{3}}}V_{c_{\alpha_{3}}c_{\alpha_{4}}}P^{(o)}_{l_{\alpha_{4}}l_{\alpha_{5}}}\cdots V_{c_{\alpha_{2k-1}}c_{\alpha_{2k}}}P^{(o)}_{l_{\alpha_{2k}}j},

(5.10)

where
V_{c_{\alpha_{2j-1}}c_{\alpha_{2j}}}
is one of the two terms
(\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(\mathbb{T})}-m_{\rm sc})
or
\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(\mathbb{T})}
.

Next, we show that the summand in (
5.10
) is zero, unless there exists some
\alpha_{2j-1}\neq\alpha_{2j}
. Otherwise the summand simplifies to

\displaystyle P^{(o)}_{il_{\alpha_{1}}}V_{c_{\alpha_{1}}c_{\alpha_{1}}}P^{(o)}_{l_{\alpha_{1}}l_{\alpha_{3}}}V_{c_{\alpha_{3}}c_{\alpha_{3}}}P^{(o)}_{l_{\alpha_{3}}l_{\alpha_{5}}}\cdots V_{c_{\alpha_{2k-1}}c_{\alpha_{2k-1}}}P^{(o)}_{l_{\alpha_{2k-1}}j}.

(5.11)

For the sequence of indices
i,l_{\alpha_{1}},l_{\alpha_{3}},\cdots,l_{\alpha_{2k-1}},j
, there exists some adjacent pairs, say
(l_{\alpha_{2j-1}},l_{\alpha_{2j+1}})
, which are in different connected components of
{\mathcal{T}}^{(o)}
. Then
P^{(o)}_{l_{\alpha_{2j-1}}l_{\alpha_{2j+1}}}=0
, and the term in (
5.11
) vanishes. Consequently, for the summation (
5.10
), we can restrict to the case that there exists some
\alpha_{2j-1}\neq\alpha_{2j}
. By the same argument as in the proof of
Lemma

4.1
, using (
4.9
) and Theorem
2.4
, we can bound (
5.10
) as

\displaystyle|\eqref{e:PUP2}|\lesssim\frac{1}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta}|\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(\mathbb{T})}|,

(5.12)

and by plugging (
5.9
),(
5.10
) and (
5.12
) back into (
5.8
) we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})|\widetilde{G}^{(o)}_{ij}|^{2}\Pi_{p}\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta}\mathbb{E}\left[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})|\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}|^{2}\Pi_{p}\right].

(5.13)

Next, we will rewrite
|\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(\mathbb{T})}|
back into the Green’s function of the graph
{\mathcal{G}}
. As we do so, any term that we can bound as
\OO(\Phi+N^{-1+{\mathfrak{c}}})
is negligible, as the resulting term
\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)}(\Phi+N^{-1+{\mathfrak{c}}})\Pi_{p}\right]=\OO(\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}])
.
We denote
\mathbb{W}:=\{b_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
. We can reduce
\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}
to a function of
G
by deleting
\mathbb{W}
. This is because as
\mathbb{T}
is already deleted, deleting
\mathbb{W}
will remove all other edges affected by the switch. To see the effect, we define a new matrix
\widetilde{B}:=\widetilde{H}_{\mathbb{W}\mathbb{W}^{\complement}}
, which is the adjacency matrix of edges from
\mathbb{W}
to
\mathbb{W}^{\complement}
in the switched graph
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}
. We can then do the decomposition using Schur complement formula

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}|_{\mathbb{W}^{\complement}}&=\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}+\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}\widetilde{B}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}|_{\mathbb{W}}\widetilde{B}^{\top}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}\\
&=G^{(\mathbb{T})}|_{\mathbb{W}^{\complement}}-G^{(\mathbb{T})}(G^{(\mathbb{T})}|_{\mathbb{W}})^{-1}G^{(\mathbb{T})}+G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}\widetilde{B}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}|_{\mathbb{W}}\widetilde{B}^{\top}G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}.\end{split}

(5.14)

Thanks to
Lemma

5.2
, the contributions from the first two terms in (
5.14
), are bounded by
\OO(\Phi)
.

For the last term in (
5.14
) we write it explicitly as

\displaystyle(G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}\widetilde{B}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}|_{\mathbb{W}}\widetilde{B}^{\top}G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})})_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}=\sum_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}(G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}\widetilde{B})_{c_{\alpha}b_{\gamma}}(\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})})_{b_{\gamma}b_{\gamma^{\prime}}}(G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}\widetilde{B})_{c_{\beta}b_{\gamma^{\prime}}},

(5.15)

For the first term in the summand of (
5.15
), it is given by

\displaystyle(G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}\widetilde{B})_{c_{\alpha}b_{\gamma}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d-1}}\left(G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}_{c_{\alpha}a_{\gamma}}+\sum_{x\sim b_{\gamma}\atop x\neq c_{\gamma}}G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}_{c_{\alpha}x}\right)

(5.16)

where the adjacency relation
\sim
is with respect to the graph
{\mathcal{G}}
. Plugging (
5.16
) into (
5.15
), and using
Lemma

5.2
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\bf S}}\left[\left|\sum_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in[\![{\mu}]\!]\atop\gamma\neq\alpha}(G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}\widetilde{B})_{c_{\alpha}b_{\gamma}}(\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})})_{b_{\gamma}b_{\gamma^{\prime}}}(G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}\widetilde{B})_{c_{\beta}b_{\gamma^{\prime}}}\right|^{2}\right]\lesssim\Phi.

A similar estimate holds for
\gamma^{\prime}\neq\beta
. Thus we can reduce (
5.15
) to the sum where
\alpha=\gamma,\beta=\gamma^{\prime}
