\displaystyle\frac{1}{d-1}\left(G_{c_{\alpha}a_{\alpha}}^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}+\sum_{x\sim b_{\alpha}\atop x\neq c_{\alpha}}G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}_{c_{\alpha}x}\right)\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}_{b_{\alpha}b_{\beta}}\left(G_{c_{\beta}a_{\beta}}^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}+\sum_{y\sim b_{\beta}\atop y\neq c_{\beta}}G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}_{yc_{\beta}}\right).

(5.17)

Next, we show that we can replace
G_{c_{\alpha}x}^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})},G_{yc_{\beta}}^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}
in the above expression by
G_{c_{\alpha}x}^{(b_{\alpha})},G_{yc_{\beta}}^{(b_{\beta})}
, and further reduce (
5.17
) to

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}\left|\left(G_{c_{\alpha}a_{\alpha}}^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}+\sum_{x\sim b_{\alpha}\atop x\neq c_{\alpha}}G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}_{c_{\alpha}x}\right)\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}_{b_{\alpha}b_{\beta}}\left(G_{c_{\beta}a_{\beta}}^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}+\sum_{y\sim b_{\beta}\atop y\neq c_{\beta}}G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}_{yc_{\beta}}\right)\right|^{2}\\
&\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}\left|\sum_{{x\sim b_{\alpha},y\sim b_{\beta}\atop x\neq a_{\alpha},y\neq a_{\beta}}}G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{c_{\alpha}x}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}_{b_{\alpha}b_{\beta}}G^{(b_{\beta})}_{yc_{\beta}}\right|^{2}+\OO(\Phi),\end{split}

(5.18)

where the two terms
G_{c_{\alpha}a_{\alpha}}^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}
and
G_{c_{\beta}a_{\beta}}^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}
are negligible thanks to
Lemma

5.2
. To replace
G_{c_{\alpha}x}^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}
by
G_{c_{\alpha}x}^{(b_{\alpha})}
, we use the following Schur complement formula

\displaystyle G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}_{c_{\alpha}x}=G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{c_{\alpha}x}-\sum_{u,v\in\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W}\backslash b_{\alpha}}G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{c_{\alpha}u}(G|_{\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W}}^{(b_{\alpha})})^{-1}_{uv}G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{vx},

then the last term can be bounded using the Ward identity

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[|G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{c_{\alpha}u}|^{2}]\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}\left[|G_{c_{\alpha}u}|^{2}+\frac{|G_{c_{\alpha}b_{\alpha}}G_{b_{\alpha}u}|^{2}}{|G_{b_{\alpha}b_{\alpha}}|^{2}}\right]\lesssim\Phi.

(5.19)

Finally for (
5.18
), we notice that
c_{\alpha},x
are distinct neighbors of
b_{\alpha}
; and
y,c_{\beta}
are distinct neighbors of
b_{\beta}
. We use the upper bound
\varepsilon
from Theorem
2.4
on
G_{yc_{\beta}}^{(b_{\beta})}
and the loose bound of
|\widetilde{G}_{b_{\alpha}b_{\beta}}^{(\mathbb{T})}|\lesssim 1
, which gives

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\mathbb{E}I(o,{\mathcal{G}})\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}\left[\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\sim b_{\alpha},y\sim b_{\beta}\\
x\neq a_{\alpha},y\neq a_{\beta}\end{subarray}}G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{c_{\alpha}x}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}_{b_{\alpha}b_{\beta}}G^{(b_{\beta})}_{yc_{\beta}}\right|^{2}\Pi_{p}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{x\sim b_{\alpha}x\neq a_{\alpha}}\varepsilon^{2}\mathbb{E}[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[|G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{c_{\alpha}x}|^{2}]\Pi_{p}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{x\sim b_{\alpha}x\neq a_{\alpha}}\varepsilon^{2}\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[I(c_{\alpha},{\mathcal{G}})|G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{c_{\alpha}x}|^{2}]\Pi_{p}]+N^{-1+{\mathfrak{c}}}\mathbb{E}[\Pi_{p}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}\mathbb{E}[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})|G^{(o)}_{ij}|^{2}\Pi_{p}]+\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}]

where in the third line we used that
\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[1-I(c_{\alpha},{\mathcal{G}})]\leqslant N^{-1+{\mathfrak{c}}}
; in the last line, we used the permutation invariance of the vertices, so that
G_{ij}^{(o)}
and
G_{c_{\alpha}x}^{(b_{\alpha})}
have the same distribution.

Thus combining the discussion above, we arrive at the following bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})|G_{ij}^{(o)}|^{2}\Pi_{p}]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}]+\varepsilon^{2}\mathbb{E}[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})|G_{ij}^{(o)}|^{2}\Pi_{p}],

(5.20)

and the claim (
5.2
) follows from rearranging.

∎

As a consequence of
Proposition

5.1
, the following proposition states that during the local resampling, the errors
{\mathcal{E}}
from
Lemma

4.1
and
Lemma

4.2
(after averaging) are negligible.

Proposition 5.3
.

Fix
z\in{\mathbf{D}}
, a
d
-regular graph
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z)
and
\mathbb{T}
the vertex set of
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}(o,{\mathcal{G}})
. Let
\Pi_{p}(z)
be as in (
5.1
). We denote the indicator function
I(o,{\mathcal{G}})=1
if for any
v\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}(o,{\mathcal{G}})
,
v
has a radius
{\mathfrak{R}}
tree neighborhood. We denote
T_{\bf S}
the local resampling around
o
with resampling data
\bf S
, and
\widetilde{G}=T_{\bf S}G
. Then for any
\alpha,\beta\in[\![{\mu}]\!]
, the following holds

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{N}\sum_{u\not\in\mathbb{T}}\mathbb{E}\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})|\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T}\cup\mathbb{X})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}-G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}|\Pi_{p}]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}],\quad\mathbb{X}=\emptyset\text{ or }\{u\},\\
&\frac{1}{N}\sum_{u\sim v\not\in\mathbb{T}}\mathbb{E}\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})|\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T}\cup\mathbb{X})}_{c_{\alpha}v}-G_{c_{\alpha}v}^{(b_{\alpha}\cup\mathbb{X})}|\Pi_{p}]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}],\quad\mathbb{X}=\emptyset\text{ or }\{u\},\\
&\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{u\sim v\not\in\mathbb{T}}\sum_{u^{\prime}\sim v^{\prime}\not\in\mathbb{T}}\mathbb{E}\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})|\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T}uu^{\prime})}_{vv^{\prime}}-G^{uu^{\prime}}_{vv^{\prime}}|\Pi_{p}]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}],\end{split}

(5.21)

where the adjacency relation
\sim
is with respect to the graph
{\mathcal{G}}
.

Proof of Proposition
5.3
.

We will only prove the first statement in (
5.21
) with
\alpha\neq\beta
and
\mathbb{X}=\emptyset
as the others are similar.
Thanks to the Schur complement formula, similar to (
5.14
), we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}-G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}=-G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}(G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}|_{\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W}\backslash\{b_{\alpha}b_{\beta}\}})^{-1}G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}+G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}\widetilde{B}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}|_{\mathbb{W}}\widetilde{B}^{\top}G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}.

(5.22)

where
\mathbb{W}:=\{b_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
and
\widetilde{B}:=\widetilde{H}_{\mathbb{W}\mathbb{W}^{\complement}}
.
Therefore, for
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})|\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}-G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}|]\\
&\leqslant\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})|(G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}(G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}|_{\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W}\backslash\{b_{\alpha}b_{\beta}\}})^{-1}G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})})_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}|]\\
&+\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})|(G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}\widetilde{B}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}|_{\mathbb{W}}\widetilde{B}^{\top}G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})})_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}|].\end{split}

(5.23)

We consider the two error terms separately. By a Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}\bigg[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})|(G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}(G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}|_{\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W}\backslash\{b_{\alpha}b_{\beta}\}})^{-1}G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})})_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}|\bigg]\\
&\lesssim\sum_{x,y\in\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W}\setminus\{b_{\alpha},b_{\beta}\}}\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})|G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}_{c_{\alpha}x}|^{2}|(G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}|_{\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W}\setminus\{b_{\alpha}b_{\beta}\}})_{xy}^{-1}|]\\
&\phantom{{}\lesssim\sum_{x,y\in\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W}\setminus\{b_{\alpha},b_{\beta}\}}{}}+\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})|G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}_{yc_{\beta}}|^{2}(G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}|_{\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W}\setminus\{b_{\alpha}b_{\beta}\}})_{xy}^{-1}|]\\
&\lesssim\sum_{x,y\in\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W}\setminus\{b_{\alpha},b_{\beta}\}}\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})|(G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}_{c_{\alpha}x}|^{2}+|G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}_{yc_{\beta}}|^{2})]\lesssim\Phi,\end{split}

(5.24)

where in the last line we used that
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega
, and the row and column sums of
(G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}|_{\mathbb{T}W\backslash b_{\alpha}b_{\beta}})^{-1}
are of order
\OO(1)
. Then, using a Ward identity as in (
5.19
), the above sum is bounded by
\OO(\Phi)
as desired.

For the second error term in (
5.22
), we need to bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}\left[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})|(G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}\widetilde{B}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}|_{\mathbb{W}}\widetilde{B}^{\top}G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})})_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}|\right].

(5.25)

By the same analysis as for (
5.15
), we can expand this term out and use the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[|(G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}\widetilde{B}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}|_{\mathbb{W}}\widetilde{B}^{\top}G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})})_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}]\lesssim\sum_{\gamma\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[|(G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}\widetilde{B})_{c_{\alpha}b_{\gamma}}|^{2}]+\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[|(G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}\widetilde{B})_{c_{\beta}b_{\gamma}}|^{2}].

The two terms above can be studied in the same way, in the following we will focus on the first term. If
\gamma\neq\alpha
, thanks to
Lemma

5.2
, we have
\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[|(G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}\widetilde{B})_{c_{\alpha}b_{\gamma}}|^{2}]\lesssim\Phi
. For
\gamma=\alpha
, then by the same argument as for (
5.18
), we can show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[|(G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}\widetilde{B})_{c_{\alpha}b_{\alpha}}|^{2}]\lesssim\sum_{x\sim b_{\alpha}\atop x\neq b_{\alpha}}\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[|G_{c_{\alpha}x}^{(b_{\alpha})}|^{2}]+\OO(\Phi).

Combining the above estimates, and taking expectation on both sides of (
5.25
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\mathbb{E}\left[I(o,{\mathcal{G}})\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[|(G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})}\widetilde{B}\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}|_{\mathbb{W}}\widetilde{B}^{\top}G^{(\mathbb{T}\mathbb{W})})_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}]|\Pi_{p}\right]\\
&\lesssim\sum_{x\sim b_{\alpha}\atop x\neq b_{\alpha}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[|G_{c_{\alpha}x}^{(b_{\alpha})}|^{2}]\Pi_{p}]+\mathbb{E}[\Phi\Pi_{p}]\\
&\lesssim\sum_{x\sim b_{\alpha}\atop x\neq b_{\alpha}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[I(c_{\alpha},{\mathcal{G}})|G_{c_{\alpha}x}^{(b_{\alpha})}|^{2}]\Pi_{p}]+\mathbb{E}[\Phi\Pi_{p}]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}],\end{split}

(5.26)

where in the third inequality, we used that with probability at least
1-N^{{\mathfrak{c}}-1}
,
I(c_{\alpha},{\mathcal{G}})=1
; in the last inequality we used
Proposition

5.1
and the permutation invariance of the vertices.

The claim (
5.21
) follows from combining (
5.23
), (
5.24
) and (
5.26
).

∎

We now give the general ways of bounding the expectation of generic terms (recall from
Definition

3.7
.

Proposition 5.4
.

Given a generic term
R_{\bf i}
as in
Definition

3.7
, and recall
\Pi_{p}(z)
from (
5.1
), then for any function
V(Q,m,z)
of
Q(z),m(z),z
, the following holds

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|V(Q,m,z)||R_{\bf i}|]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[|V(Q,m,z)|\Pi_{p}].

(5.27)

Moreover, if
R_{\bf i}
satisfies one of the following two conditions

1.

R_{\bf i}
contains two terms in the form:
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{cc^{\prime}}^{(bb^{\prime})}
with
(c,b)\neq(c^{\prime},b^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}
;

2.

R_{\bf i}
contains a term
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{cc^{\prime}}^{(bb^{\prime})}
with

(c,b)\neq(c^{\prime},b^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}
, and a Green’s function term
(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{ss^{\prime}}-P_{ss^{\prime}})
with
s,s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{K}}
in different connected components of
{\mathcal{F}}
;

3.

R_{\bf i}
contains a factor
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{cc^{\prime}}^{(bb^{\prime})}
with
(c,b)\neq(c^{\prime},b^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}
, and
{\mathcal{V}}\neq\emptyset
.

then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)R_{\bf i}]=\OO(\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}]).

(5.28)

Proof.

Based on
Definition

3.7
, assume that there are
k
special edges
{\mathcal{V}}=\{(u_{i},v_{i})\}_{1\leqslant i\leqslant k}
, for
0\leqslant k\leqslant 2p-1
. Then we can write

R_{\bf i}=(Q-Y_{\ell}(Q))^{2p-1-k}(1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(Q))^{k}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{k}G_{s_{i}w_{i}}G_{s^{\prime}_{i}w_{i}^{\prime}}\right)W,

for some
s_{i},s_{i}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{K}}
and
w_{i},w_{i}^{\prime}\in\{u_{i},v_{i}\}
corresponding to the special edge
(u_{i},v_{i})
, and
W
is the product of other factors. For
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega
, thanks to
Theorem

2.4
we have
|W|\lesssim 1
. Then we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|V||R_{\bf i}|]\\
&=\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|V|\left|(Q-Y_{\ell}(Q))^{2p-1-k}(1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(Q))^{k}\prod_{i=1}^{k}G_{s_{i}w_{i}}G_{s^{\prime}_{i}w_{i}^{\prime}}W\right|\right]\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|V||Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|^{2p-1-k}\prod_{i=1}^{k}(|G_{s_{i}w_{i}}|^{2}+|G_{s^{\prime}_{i}w_{i}^{\prime}}|^{2})].\end{split}

(5.29)

We can then first sum over the special edges, thanks to the Ward identity, each special edge gives a factor
N^{\mathfrak{c}}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m]/(N\eta)=\Phi
. Then we sum over the remaining indices in
{\bf i}
. This gives (
5.27
)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|V||R_{\bf i}|]\lesssim\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|V|\left(|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|+\Phi\right)^{2p-1}\right].

Next we prove (
5.28
) under assumption
Item

1
. We start with the Schur complement formula

\displaystyle G_{cc^{\prime}}^{(bb^{\prime})}=G_{cc^{\prime}}+(G(G|_{\{bb^{\prime}\}})^{-1}G)_{cc^{\prime}}.

(5.30)

For the last term in (
5.30
), it consists of four terms

\displaystyle G_{cb}(G|_{\{bb^{\prime}\}})_{bb^{\prime}}^{-1}G_{b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}+G_{cb}(G|_{\{bb^{\prime}\}})_{bb}^{-1}G_{bc^{\prime}}+G_{cb^{\prime}}(G|_{\{bb^{\prime}\}})_{b^{\prime}b}^{-1}G_{bc^{\prime}}+G_{cb^{\prime}}(G|_{\{bb^{\prime}\}})_{b^{\prime}b^{\prime}}^{-1}G_{b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}

(5.31)

For
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega
, thanks to
Theorem

2.4
, we conclude that

\displaystyle|G_{cc^{\prime}}^{(bb^{\prime})}|\lesssim|G_{bb^{\prime}}|+|G_{bc^{\prime}}|+|G_{b^{\prime}c}|+|G_{cc^{\prime}}|,

and the Ward identity leads to

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{(Nd)^{2}}\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\sum_{c\sim b\atop c^{\prime}\sim b^{\prime}}|G_{cc^{\prime}}^{(bb^{\prime})}|^{2}&\lesssim\frac{1}{(Nd)^{2}}\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\sum_{c\sim b\atop c^{\prime}\sim b^{\prime}}(|G_{bb^{\prime}}|^{2}+|G_{bc^{\prime}}|^{2}+|G_{b^{\prime}c}|^{2}+|G_{cc^{\prime}}|^{2})\\
&\lesssim\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\Phi.\end{split}

(5.32)

Under assumption
Item

1
that
R_{\bf i}
contains two Green’s function terms
G_{c_{1}c^{\prime}_{1}}^{(b_{1}b^{\prime}_{1})}
,
G_{c_{2}c^{\prime}_{2}}^{(b_{2}b^{\prime}_{2})}
, where
(b_{1},c_{1})
,
(b_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime})
,
(b_{2},c_{2}),(b_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{K}}
. The same as in (
5.29
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\left|\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)R_{\bf i}]\right|\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\left|\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|^{2p-1-k}(|G_{c_{1}c^{\prime}_{1}}^{(b_{1}b^{\prime}_{1})}|^{2}+|G_{c_{2}c^{\prime}_{2}}^{(b_{2}b^{\prime}_{2})}|^{2})\prod_{i=1}^{k}(|G_{s_{i}w_{i}}|^{2}+|G_{s^{\prime}_{i}w_{i}^{\prime}}|^{2})\right]\right|.\end{split}

(5.33)

Then can then first sum over the special edges, which gives a factor
\Phi^{k}
. Then thanks to (
5.33
), the average over
b_{1},b_{1}^{\prime},c_{1},c_{1},b_{2},b_{2}^{\prime},c_{2},c_{2}^{\prime}
gives another factor of
\Phi
. Finally we sum over the remaining indices in
{\bf i}
. This gives (
5.28
):

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|R_{\bf i}|]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\Phi\left(\Phi+|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|\right)^{2p-1}\right]=\OO(\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}]).

The statement under assumption
Item

2
can be proven in the same way as above, so we omit.

Next we prove (
5.28
) under assumption
Item

3
that
R_{\bf i}
contains a term
(d-1)^{2\ell}G^{(b_{1}b_{2})}_{c_{1}c_{2}}
with
(b_{1},c_{1})\neq(b_{2},c_{2})\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}
. We define
{\mathcal{X}}_{1}=(b_{1},c_{1})
and
{\mathcal{X}}_{2}=(b_{2},c_{2})
. They are two distinct connected components of
{\mathcal{F}}
. If there is another Green’s function term
(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{ss^{\prime}}-P_{ss^{\prime}})
with
s\in{\mathcal{X}}_{1},s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{2}
, or another copy
(d-1)^{2\ell}G^{(b_{1}b_{2})}_{c_{1}c_{2}}
, then the same argument as in
Item

1
and
Item

2
gives (
5.28
).

We then consider the case that
(d-1)^{2\ell}G^{(b_{1}b_{2})}_{c_{1}c_{2}}
is the unique term which contains indices in both
{\mathcal{X}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{X}}_{2}
. We will do this by categorizing factors of
R_{\bf i}
based on their relationship with
{\mathcal{X}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{X}}_{2}
. To see how to categorize factors, we consider
U,V
as vectors with entries
U(b,c)
and
V(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\frac{1}{N^{2}d^{2}}\sum_{b\sim c\atop b^{\prime}\sim c^{\prime}}U(b,c)G_{cc}^{(bb^{\prime})}V(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})\right|&=\left|\frac{1}{N^{2}d^{2}}\sum_{b\sim c\atop b^{\prime}\sim c^{\prime}}U(b,c)(G_{cc^{\prime}}+(G(G|_{\{bb^{\prime}\}})^{-1}G)_{cc^{\prime}})V(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})\right|,\end{split}

(5.34)

where the adjacency
\sim
is with respect to the graph
{\mathcal{G}}
.
Because
\|G(E+\mathrm{i}\eta)\|_{\spec}\leqslant\eta^{-1}
, then

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{N^{2}d^{2}}\sum_{b\sim c\atop b^{\prime}\sim c^{\prime}}U(b,c)G_{cc^{\prime}}V(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{2}\eta}\sqrt{\sum_{b\sim c}|U(b,c)|^{2}\sum_{b^{\prime}\sim c^{\prime}}|V(b^{\prime},c^{\prime}))|^{2}}.

(5.35)

Using the expression (
5.31
) and
Theorem

2.4
, the summation in (
5.34
) involving the term
(G(G|_{\{bb^{\prime}\}})^{-1}G)_{cc^{\prime}}
gives the same bound as in (
5.35
). And we conclude that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{N^{2}d^{2}}\sum_{b\sim c\atop b^{\prime}\sim c^{\prime}}U(b,c)G_{cc}^{(bb^{\prime})}V(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{2}\eta}\sqrt{\sum_{b\sim c}|U(b,c)|^{2}\sum_{b^{\prime}\sim c^{\prime}}|V(b^{\prime},c^{\prime}))|^{2}}.

(5.36)

Denote the forest
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}
from
{\mathcal{F}}
by removing
\{(b_{1},c_{1}),(b_{2},c_{2})\}
and special edges
{\mathcal{V}}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{F}}=(\widehat{\bf i},\widehat{E})={\mathcal{F}}\setminus\left(\{(b_{1},c_{1}),(b_{2},c_{2})\}\cup\{(u_{i},v_{i})\}_{1\leqslant i\leqslant k}\right).

Next we show that we can replace the indicator function
I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})
by
I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})A_{b_{1}c_{1}}A_{b_{2}c_{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}A_{u_{i}v_{i}}
, and the error is negligible:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)R_{\bf i}]\\
&=\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})A_{b_{1}c_{1}}A_{b_{2}c_{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}A_{u_{i}v_{i}}R_{\bf i}\right]+\OO(\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}]).\end{split}

(5.37)

We denote that

\displaystyle\Delta I

\displaystyle:=I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})A_{b_{1}c_{1}}A_{b_{2}c_{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}A_{u_{i}v_{i}}-I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})

\displaystyle=\left(I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})A_{b_{1}c_{1}}A_{b_{1}c_{2}}-I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{G}})\right)\prod_{i=1}^{k}A_{u_{i}v_{i}},

where
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{F}}\setminus\{(u_{i},v_{i})\}_{i\in[\![{k}]\!]}
.
To show (
5.37
), we first notice that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta I|\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|R_{\bf i}|\right]\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta I|\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|^{2p-1-k}\prod_{i=1}^{k}(|G_{s_{i}w_{i}}|^{2}+|G_{s^{\prime}_{i}w_{i}^{\prime}}|^{2})\right]\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{Z_{\widehat{\mathcal{F}}}}\sum_{\widehat{\bf i}}\frac{1}{(Nd)^{2}}\sum_{b_{1},c_{1},b_{2},c_{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})A_{b_{1}c_{1}}A_{b_{1}c_{2}}-I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{G}})\right|\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|^{2p-1-k}\Phi^{k}\right],\end{split}

(5.38)

where
Z_{\mathcal{F}}=Z_{\widehat{\mathcal{F}}}(Nd)^{2+k}
.
For the indicator function
|I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})A_{b_{1}c_{1}}A_{b_{1}c_{2}}-I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{G}})|
in the last line of (
5.38
), it equals one if there exists
v\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}(c_{1},{\mathcal{G}})\cup{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}(c_{2},{\mathcal{G}})
, such that
v
does not have a radius
{\mathfrak{R}}
tree neighborhood; or
c_{1},c_{2}
are within distance
3{\mathfrak{R}}
to each other, or other core edges. By the same argument as in
Lemma

2.10
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(Nd)^{2}}\sum_{b_{1},c_{1},b_{2},c_{2}}|I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})A_{b_{1}c_{1}}A_{b_{1}c_{2}}-I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{G}})|\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{1-2{\mathfrak{c}}}}.

(5.39)

By plugging (
5.39
) into (
5.38
), and averaging over the indices
\widehat{\bf i}
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta I|\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|R_{\bf i}|\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{1-2{\mathfrak{c}}}}\mathbb{E}[{\bf 1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|^{2p-1-k}\Phi^{k}]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}].

Next, we study (
5.37
). We define
U(b,c),V(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})
as follows such that

\displaystyle R_{{\bf i}}=U(b_{1},c_{1})V(b_{2},c_{2})G^{(b_{1}b_{2})}_{c_{1}c_{2}}R^{\prime}_{\widehat{\bf i}},

(5.40)

where
R^{\prime}_{\widehat{\bf i}}
only depends on the indices
\widehat{\bf i}
. In particular it does not depends on indices
b_{1},c_{1},b_{2},c_{2}
and vertices in special edges.

For each special edge
(u_{i},v_{i})
, there are at least two factors in the form
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{ss^{\prime}}
with
s\in{\mathcal{K}}
and
s^{\prime}\in\{u_{i},v_{i}\}
, and possibly some factors in the form
G_{u_{i}u_{i}},G_{u_{i}v_{i}},1/G_{v_{i}v_{i}}
. We divide the set
[\![{k}]\!]=\mathbb{V}_{0}\cup\mathbb{V}_{1}\cup\mathbb{V}_{2}
:

1.

If all the factors
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{ss^{\prime}}
for
s^{\prime}\in\{u_{i},v_{i}\}
satisfies
s\not\in\{b_{2},c_{2}\}
, we put
i\in\mathbb{V}_{1}
, and all the Green’s function terms associated with
(u_{i},v_{i})
in
\widehat{U}(b_{1},c_{1})
.

2.

If
i\notin\mathbb{V}_{1}
, and all the factors
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{ss^{\prime}}
for
s^{\prime}\in\{u_{i},v_{i}\}
satisfies
s\not\in\{b_{1},c_{1}\}
, we put
i\in\mathbb{V}_{2}
, and all the Green’s function terms associated with
(u_{i},v_{i})
in
\widehat{U}(b_{2},c_{2})
.

3.

For the remaining indices
i\in\mathbb{V}_{0}
, there are some factors
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{ss^{\prime}}
with
s\in\{b_{1},c_{1}\},s^{\prime}\in\{u_{i},v_{i}\}
, we put them in
\widehat{U}^{\prime}(b_{1},c_{1})
; and some factors
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{ss^{\prime}}
with
s\in\{b_{2},c_{2}\},s^{\prime}\in\{u_{i},v_{i}\}
we put them in
\widehat{V}^{\prime}(b_{2},c_{2})
. We also put all the factors
G_{u_{i}u_{i}},G_{u_{i}v_{i}},1/G_{v_{i}v_{i}}
in
\widehat{U}^{\prime}(b_{1},c_{1})
.

For terms involving only vertices
b_{1},c_{1}
, i.e.
(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{c_{1}c_{1}}^{(b_{1})}-Q)
and
(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{b_{1}c_{1}}-P_{b_{1}c_{1}})
, we put them in
\widehat{U}(b_{1},c_{1})
; and terms involving only vertices
b_{2},c_{2}
, we put them in
\widehat{V}(b_{2},c_{2})
. In this way all the factors involving the indices
b_{1},c_{1},b_{2},c_{2}
and vertices in special edges are collected in
U(b_{1},c_{1})=\widehat{U}(b_{1},c_{1})\widehat{U}^{\prime}(b_{1},c_{1})
, and
V(b_{1},c_{1})=\widehat{V}(b_{1},c_{1})\widehat{V}^{\prime}(b_{1},c_{1})
. We collect remaining terms in
R^{\prime}_{\widehat{\bf i}}
, and this leads to (
5.40
).

Thanks to the Ward identity, and
Theorem

2.4
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{u_{i}\sim v_{i}:i\in\mathbb{V}_{1}}|\widehat{U}(b_{1},c_{1})|\lesssim(Nd\Phi)^{|\mathbb{V}_{1}|},\quad\sum_{u_{i}\sim v_{i}:i\in\mathbb{V}_{2}}|\widehat{V}(b_{2},c_{2})|\lesssim(Nd\Phi)^{|\mathbb{V}_{2}|},\\
&\sum_{u_{i}\sim v_{i}:i\in\mathbb{V}_{0}}|\widehat{U}^{\prime}(b_{1},c_{1})|^{2}\lesssim(Nd\Phi)^{|\mathbb{V}_{0}|},\quad\sum_{u_{i}\sim v_{i}:i\in\mathbb{V}_{0}}|\widehat{V}^{\prime}(b_{2},c_{2})|^{2}\lesssim(Nd\Phi)^{|\mathbb{V}_{0}|}.\end{split}

(5.41)

With the above notations, we can rewrite the righthand side of (
5.37
) as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})A_{b_{1}c_{1}}A_{b_{2}c_{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}A_{u_{i}v_{i}}R_{\bf i}\right]\\
&=\frac{1}{Z_{\widehat{\mathcal{F}}}}\sum_{\widehat{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})}{(Nd)^{k+2}}\sum_{u_{i}\sim v_{i}:i\in\mathbb{V}_{0}}R_{\widehat{\bf i}}\sum_{b_{1}\sim c_{1}\atop b_{2}\sim c_{2}}\left(\sum_{u_{i}\sim v_{i}:i\in\mathbb{V}_{1}}U(b_{1},c_{1})\right)G_{c_{1}c_{2}}^{(b_{1}b_{2})}\left(\sum_{u_{i}\sim v_{i}:i\in\mathbb{V}_{2}}V(b_{2},c_{2})\right)\right].\end{split}

(5.42)

We can first sum over the special edges and
b_{1},c_{1},b_{2},c_{2}
using (
5.36
) and (
5.41
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)}{(Nd)^{k+2}}\sum_{u_{i}\sim v_{i}:i\in\mathbb{V}_{0}}\sum_{b_{1}\sim c_{1}\atop b_{2}\sim c_{2}}\left(\sum_{u_{i}\sim v_{i}:i\in\mathbb{V}_{1}}U(b_{1},c_{1})\right)G_{c_{1}c_{2}}^{(b_{1}b_{2})}\left(\sum_{u_{i}\sim v_{i}:i\in\mathbb{V}_{2}}V(b_{2},c_{2})\right)\\
&\leqslant\frac{\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)}{(Nd)^{k+2}}\sum_{u_{i}\sim v_{i}:i\in\mathbb{V}_{0}}\frac{1}{\eta}\sqrt{\sum_{b_{1}\sim c_{1}}\left|\sum_{u_{i}\sim v_{i}:i\in\mathbb{V}_{1}}U(b_{1},c_{1})\right|^{2}\sum_{b_{2}\sim c_{2}}\left|\sum_{u_{i}\sim v_{i}:i\in\mathbb{V}_{2}}V(b_{2},c_{2})\right|^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)}{(Nd)^{k+2}}\frac{1}{\eta}(Nd\Phi)^{|\mathbb{V}_{1}|+|\mathbb{V}_{2}|}\sum_{u_{i}\sim v_{i}:i\in\mathbb{V}_{0}}\left(\sum_{b_{1}\sim c_{1}}|\widehat{U}(b_{1},c_{1})|^{2}+\sum_{b_{1}\sim c_{1}}|\widehat{U}(b_{1},c_{1})|^{2}\right)\\
&\lesssim\frac{\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)}{(Nd)^{k+2}}\frac{1}{\eta}(Nd\Phi)^{|\mathbb{V}_{0}|+|\mathbb{V}_{1}|+|\mathbb{V}_{2}|}=\frac{\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)}{\eta(Nd)^{2}}\Phi^{k}.\end{split}

(5.43)

Then we can plug (
5.43
) into (
5.42
) to conclude

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})A_{b_{1}c_{1}}A_{b_{2}c_{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}A_{u_{i}v_{i}}R_{\bf i}\right]\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{2}\eta}\frac{1}{Z_{\widehat{\mathcal{F}}}}\sum_{\widehat{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})|R_{\widehat{\bf i}}|\Phi^{k}\right]\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{2}\eta}\frac{1}{Z_{\widehat{\mathcal{F}}}}\sum_{\widehat{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|^{2p-1-k}|1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(Q)|^{k}\Phi^{k}\right]\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{2}\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|^{2p-1-k}|1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(Q)|^{k}\Phi^{k}\right]\\
&\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)(|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|+\Phi)^{2p-2}\Phi\frac{|1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(Q)|\Phi}{N\eta}\right]=\OO(\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}]).\end{split}

(5.44)

The two estimates (
5.42
) and (
5.44
) together lead to (
5.28
) under assumption
Item

3
.
∎

6
Proof of the Iteration Scheme

In this section we prove
Proposition

3.9
and
Proposition

3.10
.

6.1
Proof of
Proposition

3.9

The following proposition is crucial for proving
Proposition

3.9
. It states that if an index
\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]
appears exactly once in the expression, then its expectation over the randomness of the choice of the resampling data
{\bf S}
is small.

Proposition 6.1
.

Fix
z\in{\mathbf{D}}
, a
d
-regular graph
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z)
, and an edge
(i,o)\in{\mathcal{G}}
.
We denote
T_{\bf S}
the local resampling with resampling data
{\bf S}=\{(l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha}),(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
around
o
, and
\widetilde{G}=T_{\bf S}G
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q],\quad\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[G_{b_{\alpha}s}]\quad\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[G_{c_{\alpha}s}]=\OO\left(\frac{1}{N^{1-{\mathfrak{c}}}}\right),

(6.1)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[G_{c_{\alpha}c}^{(b_{\alpha}b)}]=\frac{d}{\sqrt{d-1}}\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}\left[(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)G_{b_{\alpha}c}^{(b)}\right]+\OO\left(\frac{1}{N^{1-{\mathfrak{c}}}}+\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}\left[\sum_{v\neq v^{\prime}\sim b_{\alpha}}(|G_{vv^{\prime}}^{(b_{\alpha})}|^{2}+|G_{b_{\alpha}c}^{(b)}|^{2})\right]\right).\end{split}

(6.2)

For any vector
w\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
, and a term
U^{(u,v)}
, which is a product of
G_{vv},G_{vv},1/G_{uu}
, the following holds

\displaystyle\quad\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{s,u,v}w_{s}\mathbb{E}_{{\bf S}}[A_{uv}G_{{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha}u}G_{sv}U^{(u,v)}],\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{s,u,v}w_{s}\mathbb{E}_{{\bf S}}[A_{uv}G_{{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha}u}G_{su}U^{(u,v)}]=\OO\left(\frac{\|w\|_{2}}{N^{5/2-{\mathfrak{c}}}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]}\right),

(6.3)

for any
{\mathsf{L}}\in\{l,a,b,c\}
.

Proof of
Proposition

6.1
.

To prove (
6.1
) and (
6.3
), we first notice that the Green’s function
G
satisfies

\displaystyle\sum_{x}G_{xs}=\sum_{s}G_{xs}=\frac{1}{d/\sqrt{d-1}-z}\lesssim 1,

(6.4)

for
z\in\bf D
from (
1.7
). We recall from
Section

2.2
, the directed edge
(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})
is uniform randomly selected in
{\mathcal{G}}^{(\mathbb{T})}
, where
\mathbb{T}
is the vertex set of
{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}(o,{\mathcal{G}})
. Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q]=\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}]-Q=\frac{1}{Nd-\OO(|\mathbb{T}|)}\sum_{u\sim v}G_{vv}^{(u)}-Q+\OO\left(\frac{|\mathbb{T}|}{Nd}\right)=\OO\left(\frac{1}{N^{1-{\mathfrak{c}}}}\right),

where the sum is over all pairs of adjacent vertices
u\sim v
, and we used the definition (
1.10
) of
Q
.
Moreover, the expectation
\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[\cdot]
over
b_{\alpha}
or
c_{\alpha}
is uniform over
[\![{N}]\!]\setminus{\mathbb{X}}
where
{\mathbb{X}}=\mathbb{T}\cup\{a_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[G_{b_{\alpha}s}]=\frac{1}{N-\OO(|{\mathbb{X}}|)}\sum_{v}G_{vs}+\OO\left(\frac{|{\mathbb{X}}|}{N}\right)=\OO\left(\frac{1}{N^{1-{\mathfrak{c}}}}\right).

(6.5)

where we used (
6.4
). The same estimate holds for
\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[G_{c_{\alpha}s}]
.

For (
6.2
), we first notice that by the same argument as for (
6.5
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[G_{b_{\alpha}c}^{(b)}]=\OO\left(\frac{1}{N^{1-{\mathfrak{c}}}}\right).

(6.6)

Moreover, for any vertex
u\in[\![{N}]\!]
, the Schur complement formula gives

\displaystyle-\frac{G_{uu}}{\sqrt{d-1}}\sum_{v:v\sim u}G_{vc}^{(ub)}=G_{uc}^{(b)},\quad-\frac{1}{G_{uu}}=z+\frac{1}{d-1}\sum_{v,v^{\prime}\sim u}G_{vv^{\prime}}^{(u)}.

By taking
(u,v)=(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})
in the above expression, and taking expectation, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[G_{c_{\alpha}c}^{(b_{\alpha}b)}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}\left[\left(z\sqrt{d-1}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d-1}}\sum_{v,v^{\prime}\sim b_{\alpha}}G_{vv^{\prime}}^{(b_{\alpha})}\right)G_{b_{\alpha}c}^{(b)}\right]+\OO\left(\frac{1}{N^{1-{\mathfrak{c}}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{\sqrt{d-1}}\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}\left[(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)G_{b_{\alpha}c}^{(b)}\right]+{\mathcal{E}}^{(b,c)}+\OO\left(\frac{1}{N^{1-{\mathfrak{c}}}}\right),

where we used (
6.6
), and the error term
{\mathcal{E}}^{(b,c)}
is given by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}^{(b,c)}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d-1}}\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}\left[\sum_{v\neq v^{\prime}\sim b_{\alpha}}G_{vv^{\prime}}^{(b_{\alpha})}G_{b_{\alpha}c}^{(b)}\right].

This gives (
6.2
).

In the following we prove (
6.3
). We will only prove the first statement, the second one can be proven in the same way. We have

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{u,v}w_{s}\mathbb{E}_{{\bf S}}[A_{uv}G_{{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha}u}G_{sv}U^{(u,v)}]

\displaystyle=\frac{\OO(1)}{N^{2}}\sum_{x\in[\![{N}]\!]}\sum_{u,v}w_{s}A_{uv}G_{xu}G_{sv}U^{(u,v)}+\OO\left(\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{x\in{\mathbb{X}}}\sum_{u,v}|w_{s}|A_{uv}|G_{xu}G_{sv}U^{(u,v)}|\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\OO(1)}{N^{2}}\sum_{u,v}w_{s}G_{sv}A_{uv}U^{(u,v)}+\OO\left(\frac{|w_{s}|N^{\mathfrak{c}}}{N^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]}\right),

where in the last line we used (
6.4
) and the Ward identity.
If we further average over index
s
, using that
\|G\|_{\spec}\leqslant 1/\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\frac{\OO(1)}{N^{3}}\sum_{s,v}w_{s}G_{sv}\sum_{u}A_{uv}U^{(u,v)}+\OO\left(\sum_{s}\frac{|w_{s}|N^{\mathfrak{c}}}{N^{3}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{3}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]}\|w\|_{2}\sqrt{\sum_{v}\left|\sum_{u}U^{(u,v)}A_{uv}\right|^{2}}+\frac{N^{\mathfrak{c}}}{N^{3}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]}(\sqrt{N}\|w\|_{2})\lesssim\frac{\|w\|_{2}}{N^{5/2-{\mathfrak{c}}}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]}.

This gives (
6.3
).

∎

Proof of
Proposition

3.9
.

Fix an embedding of
{\mathcal{F}}
in to
{\mathcal{G}}
, such that
I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})=1
. We pick a switching edge from last step, and denote it as
(i,o)
. We will do a local resampling around the vertex
o
. We split the lefthand side of (
3.14
) into two terms

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}}){\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)}R_{\bf i}({\mathcal{G}})\right]\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})I({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}){\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)}\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\overline{\Omega})R_{\bf i}({\mathcal{G}})\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[1-I({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\overline{\Omega})]{\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)}R_{\bf i}({\mathcal{G}})\right]\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})I({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}){\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\overline{\Omega})}R_{\bf i}({\mathcal{G}})\right]+\OO\left(N^{-1+2{\mathfrak{c}}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}}){\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)}|R_{\bf i}({\mathcal{G}})|\right]\right),\end{split}

(6.7)

where in the third line we used
Lemma

3.5
, that
\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[1-I({\mathcal{F}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}})\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\overline{\Omega})]\leqslant N^{-1+2{\mathfrak{c}}}
. After averaging over
{\bf i}
, thanks to
Proposition

5.4
the second term on the righthand side of (
6.7
) is bounded as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}N^{-1+2{\mathfrak{c}}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}}){\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)}|R_{\bf i}({\mathcal{G}})|\right]=\OO(\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}]).

Next we analyze the first term in the last line of (
6.7
).

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})I({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\overline{\Omega})R_{\bf i}({\mathcal{G}})\right]\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})I({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}){\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)}R_{\bf i}({\mathcal{G}})\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})I({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}){\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Omega)}R_{\bf i}({\mathcal{G}})\right].\end{split}

(6.8)

For
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega
, we can bound the second term on the righthand side of (
6.8
) by the same argument as for (
5.27
), and then using (
5.7
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})I({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}){\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Omega)}|R_{\bf i}({\mathcal{G}})|\right]\\
&\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[(|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|+\Phi)^{2p-1}{\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\bm{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Omega)}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}]\end{split}

(6.9)

The main term is the first term on the righthand side of (
6.8
). Recall that
R_{\bf i}({\mathcal{G}})=(G_{oo}^{(i)}-Y_{\ell}(Q))R_{\bf i}^{\prime}({\mathcal{G}})
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})I({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)R_{\bf i}({\mathcal{G}})\right]\\
&=\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})I({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)G_{oo}^{(i)}R^{\prime}_{\bf i}({\mathcal{G}})\right]\\
&-\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})I({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)Y_{\ell}(Q)R^{\prime}_{\bf i}({\mathcal{G}})\right].\end{split}

(6.10)

For the first term on the righthand side of (
6.10
), thanks to
Lemma

2.9
,
{\mathcal{G}}
and
{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}=T_{\bf S}({\mathcal{G}})
forms an exchangeable pair, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})I({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)G_{oo}^{(i)}R^{\prime}_{\bf i}({\mathcal{G}})\right]\\
&=\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})I({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)\widetilde{G}_{oo}^{(i)}R^{\prime}_{\bf i}(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\right],\end{split}

(6.11)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})I({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)Y_{\ell}(Q)R^{\prime}_{\bf i}({\mathcal{G}})\right]\\
&=\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})I({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)Y_{\ell}(\widetilde{Q})R^{\prime}_{\bf i}(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\right]\\
&=\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})I({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)Y_{\ell}(Q)R^{\prime}_{\bf i}(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\right]\\
&+\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})I({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)(Y_{\ell}(Q)-Y_{\ell}(\widetilde{Q}))R^{\prime}_{\bf i}({\mathcal{G}})\right].\end{split}

(6.12)

For the last term on the righthand side of (
6.12
), we can bound it as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\left|\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})I({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)(Y_{\ell}(Q)-Y_{\ell}(\widetilde{Q}))R^{\prime}_{\bf i}({\mathcal{G}})\right]\right|\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)|\widetilde{Q}-Q||R^{\prime}_{\bf i}({\mathcal{G}})|\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}],\end{split}

(6.13)

where in the second line we used that
|Y^{\prime}|\lesssim 1
from (
2.9
); in the last line we used that
|\widetilde{Q}-Q|\lesssim\Phi
from
Lemma

4.6
and (
5.27
).
The estimates (
6.10
), (
6.11
) and (
6.12
) and (
6.13
) together finishes the proof of
Proposition

3.9
.
∎

6.2
Proof of
Proposition

3.10

We denote
T_{\bf S}
the local resampling with resampling data
{\bf S}=\{(l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha}),(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
around
o
, and
\widetilde{G}=T_{\bf S}G
.
We expand the forest
{\mathcal{F}}
by including the resampling data:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{F}}=(\widehat{\bf i},\widehat{E})={\mathcal{F}}\bigcup{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(o)\bigcup_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}\{(l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha}),(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\},\quad{\mathcal{K}}^{+}=\widehat{\mathcal{K}}={\mathcal{K}}\bigcup_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}\{(l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha}),(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}^{+}=\widehat{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{C}}\bigcup_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}\{(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\},\quad({\mathcal{C}}^{+})^{\circ}=\widehat{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}={\mathcal{C}}^{+}\setminus\{(i,o)\},\quad P=P(\widehat{\mathcal{F}},z,m_{\rm sc}).

By the same argument as in (
6.7
), we can remove the indicator function
I({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})
; and thanks to
Lemma

2.10
, we can further replace
{\mathcal{F}}
by
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}
as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})I({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)(\widetilde{G}_{oo}^{(i)}-Y_{\ell}(Q))R^{\prime}_{\bf i}(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\right]\\
&=\frac{1}{Z_{\widehat{\mathcal{F}}}}\sum_{\widehat{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)(\widetilde{G}_{oo}^{(i)}-Y_{\ell}(Q))R^{\prime}_{\bf i}(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\right]+\OO(\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}]).\end{split}

(6.14)

In order to use our various lemmas from Section
4
that allow us to reduce
R_{\bf i}(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})
to terms of the unswitched graph
{\mathcal{G}}
, we need to classify the factors of
R^{\prime}_{\bf i}
based on their dependence on
o,i
, where
(i,o)\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}
. We write

\displaystyle R^{\prime}_{\bf i}=\prod_{j\in J}W_{j},\quad W_{0}:=(d-1)^{2\ell}(\widetilde{G}_{oo}^{(i)}-Y_{\ell}(Q))

(6.15)

where
W_{j}
is a factor of a generic function, as defined in Definition
3.7
. Examining the definition, we see that factors are only of the form

\displaystyle\{(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{cc}^{(b)}-Q)\}_{(b,c)\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}},\quad\{(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{ss^{\prime}}-P_{ss^{\prime}})\}_{s,s^{\prime}\in\{o,i\}},\quad\{(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{oc}^{(ib)}\}_{(i,o)\neq(b,c)\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}},

\displaystyle\{(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{ss^{\prime}}-P_{ss^{\prime}})\}_{s\in\{o,i\},s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{K}}\setminus\{o,i\}},\quad\{(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{cc^{\prime}}^{(bb^{\prime})}\}_{(i,o)\neq(b,c),(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}},

\displaystyle\{(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{ss^{\prime}}-P_{ss^{\prime}})\}_{s,s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{K}}\setminus\{o,i\}},\quad\{(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{ss^{\prime}}\}_{s\in{\mathcal{K}},s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{V}}},\quad\{G_{uv},G_{vv},1/G_{uu}\}_{(u,v)\in{\mathcal{V}}},

\displaystyle(d-1)^{2\ell}(Q-m_{\rm sc}),\quad Q-Y_{\ell}(Q),\quad 1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(Q).

We will next demonstrate the effect of the switch on each of the factors mentioned above, using the results from
Section

4
. Before that, we introduce some notations for the lower-order terms created when performing a switch. These terms are of two types:
{\mathcal{D}}_{r}^{S}
for
r\geqslant 0
, which is a weighted sum of products involving at least
r
factors of the form

\displaystyle(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q),\quad(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})},\quad(d-1)^{2\ell}(Q-m_{\rm sc}).

(6.16)

The total weight is
\OO(\poly(\ell))
. As established in
Lemma

4.1
and
Lemma

4.2
, these factors are the additional terms generated by lower-order analysis when utilizing the Schur complement formulas.

The other type is
{\mathcal{D}}_{r}^{W}
, which is a weighted sum of products involving at least
r
factors of the form

\displaystyle\{(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{ss^{\prime}}-P_{ss^{\prime}})\}_{s,s^{\prime}\in\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}}.

(6.17)

The total weight is
\OO(\poly(\ell))
.
As established in
Lemma

4.5
and
Lemma

4.6
, these factors are the additional terms generated by lower-order analysis when utilizing the Woodbury formula. The exact functions can change line to line.

The advantage of introducing these error terms is that, according to
Theorem

2.4
, each of these factors is bounded by

\varepsilon
, thus an increase of
r
gives us a demonstrably smaller term. For example
(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}\in{\mathcal{D}}^{S}_{2}
, and
|(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}|\leqslant((d-1)^{2\ell}\varepsilon)^{2}
for
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega
.

For any
(b,c)\neq(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}
, we introduce the following error functions (they are error terms in
Section

4
) that satisfy

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|{\mathcal{E}}_{1}|=\sum_{\alpha}|\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}-G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}|+\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta}|\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}-G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}|\\
&|{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{(b,c)}|=\sum_{\alpha}|\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}-G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}|)+\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta}|\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}-G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}|+N^{-2}\\
&|{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{(b,c),(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})}|=\sum_{\alpha}(|\widetilde{G}_{c_{\alpha}c}^{(\mathbb{T}b)}-G_{c_{\alpha}c}^{(b_{\alpha}b)}|+|\widetilde{G}^{(\mathbb{T}bb^{\prime})}_{cc^{\prime}}-G^{(bb^{\prime})}_{cc^{\prime}}|)+(d-1)^{\ell}\sum_{\alpha}(|\widetilde{G}_{cc_{\alpha}}^{(\mathbb{T}bb^{\prime})}|^{2}+|\widetilde{G}_{c^{\prime}c_{\alpha}}^{(\mathbb{T}bb^{\prime})}|^{2})+N^{-2},\\
&|{\mathcal{E}}_{2}|=N^{-2},\quad|{\mathcal{E}}_{3}|=\Phi.\end{split}

(6.18)

Thanks to
Proposition

5.3
, the important property of the first three errors is that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\Pi_{p}]],\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{(b,c)}\Pi_{p}]],\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{(b,c),(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})}\Pi_{p}]]=\OO(\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}]).

Consequently, these error terms are small enough that any term that incorporates these error terms as a factor is negligible.

Through the lemmas in Section
4
, we see that the leading order term of a generic function on a switched graph replaces the indices of the core edge
(i,o)
with an average over the edges
\{(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
from the resampling data
{\bf S}
. The other, lower-order terms are demonstrably smaller. We must confirm this for each of the factors listed above.
In the following,
{\mathfrak{c}}_{j}
for
j\geqslant 1
will represent constants bounded by
\OO(\poly(\ell))
that can change line to line. Given the factor
W_{j}
, we will consider the corresponding term after the local resampling, denoted as
\widetilde{W}_{j}
. Then we approximate
\widetilde{W}_{j}=\widehat{W}_{j}+{\mathcal{E}}
, where
\widehat{W}_{j}
is a new function of the original Green’s function and
{\mathcal{E}}
is an error term listed above in (
6.18
). Finally we write
\widehat{W}_{j}
as a leading order term, plus a higher order term of the form containing factors
{\mathcal{D}}_{r}^{S}
or
{\mathcal{D}}_{r}^{W}
, depending on the expansion used.

1.

By Lemma
4.1
,
\widetilde{W}_{0}:=W_{0}=(d-1)^{2\ell}(\widetilde{G}_{oo}^{(i)}-Y_{\ell}(Q)
, can be written as

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}_{0}=\widehat{W}_{0}+{\mathcal{E}}_{1},\quad\widehat{W}_{0}:={\mathfrak{c}}_{1}(d-1)^{\ell}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}(G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}-Q)+{\mathfrak{c}}_{2}(d-1)^{\ell}\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}+{\mathcal{D}}_{2}^{S}.

(6.19)

where
{\mathcal{D}}_{2}^{S},{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
are as in (
6.16
) and (
6.18
).

2.

If
W_{j}=(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{oo}^{(i)}-Q)
we can write

\displaystyle(d-1)^{2\ell}(\widetilde{G}_{oo}^{(i)}-\widetilde{Q})=(d-1)^{2\ell}(\widetilde{G}_{oo}^{(i)}-Y_{\ell}(Q))+(d-1)^{2\ell}(Y_{\ell}(Q)-\widetilde{Q}),

then expand according to Lemma
4.1
. This gives that
\widetilde{W}_{j}=\widehat{W}_{j}+{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
, where

\displaystyle\widehat{W}_{j}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{c}}_{1}(d-1)^{\ell}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)+{\mathfrak{c}}_{2}(d-1)^{\ell}\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}

\displaystyle+{\mathfrak{c}}_{3}(d-1)^{2\ell}(Y_{\ell}(Q)-Q)+{\mathfrak{c}}_{4}(d-1)^{2\ell}(Q-\widetilde{Q})+{\mathcal{D}}_{2}^{S}.

A similar expansion is possible for
W_{j}\in\{(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{ss^{\prime}}-P_{ss^{\prime}})\}_{s,s^{\prime}\in\{o,i\}}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{W}_{j}

\displaystyle={{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}(G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}-Q)+{{\mathfrak{c}}_{2}}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\alpha\notin{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}(G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}-Q)

\displaystyle+{{\mathfrak{c}}_{3}}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}+{{\mathfrak{c}}_{4}}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta\in{\mathsf{A}}^{\complement}_{i}}G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}

\displaystyle+{{\mathfrak{c}}_{5}}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}\atop\beta\in{\mathsf{A}}^{\complement}_{i}}G^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}+{\mathfrak{c}}_{6}(d-1)^{2\ell}(Q-m_{\rm sc})+{\mathcal{D}}_{2}^{S}.

3.

For
W_{j}\in\{(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{oc}^{(ib)}\}_{(i,o)\neq(b,c)\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}}
, by
Lemma

4.2
, we have
\widetilde{W}_{j}=\widehat{W}_{j}+{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{(b,c)}
, where

\displaystyle\widehat{W}_{j}={\mathfrak{c}}_{1}(d-1)^{3\ell/2}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}G_{c_{\alpha}c}^{(b_{\alpha}b)}+\frac{1}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}{\mathcal{D}}_{1}^{S}\times(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{c_{\alpha}c}^{(b_{\alpha}b)},

(6.20)

where
{\mathcal{D}}_{1}^{S},{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{(b,c)}
are as in (
6.16
) and (
6.18
).

4.

For
W_{j}\in\{(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{cc}^{(b)}\}_{(b,c)\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}}
or
\{(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{cc^{\prime}}^{(bb^{\prime})}\}_{(b,c)\neq(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}}
, by
Lemma

4.2
, we can replace it as
\widetilde{W}_{j}=\widehat{W}_{j}+{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{(b,c)}
or
\widetilde{W}_{j}=\widehat{W}_{j}+{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{(b,c),(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})}
, where
\widehat{W}_{j}=W_{j}
, and
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{(b,c)},{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{(b,c),(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})}
are as in (
6.18
).

5.

For
W_{j}\in\{(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{ss^{\prime}}-P_{ss^{\prime}})\}_{s\in\{o,i\},s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{K}}\setminus\{o,i\}}
or
\{(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{ss^{\prime}}\}_{s\in\{o,i\},s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{V}}}
, by
Lemma

4.5
, we have
\widetilde{W}_{j}=\widehat{W}_{j}+{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
, where

\displaystyle\widehat{W}_{j}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{c}}_{1}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{os^{\prime}}+{\mathfrak{c}}_{2}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{is^{\prime}}+

\displaystyle+(d-1)^{3\ell/2}\left({\mathfrak{c}}_{3}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}G_{b_{\alpha}s^{\prime}}+{\mathfrak{c}}_{4}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}G_{c_{\alpha}s^{\prime}}+{\mathfrak{c}}_{5}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}^{\complement}_{i}}G_{b_{\alpha}s^{\prime}}+{\mathfrak{c}}_{6}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}^{\complement}_{i}}G_{c_{\alpha}s^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]\atop{\mathsf{L}}\in\{l,a,b,c\}}{\mathcal{D}}_{1}^{W}\times(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha}s^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(d-1)^{2\ell}}\sum_{\alpha,\beta\in[\![{\mu}]\!]\atop{\mathsf{L}},{\mathsf{L}}^{\prime}\in\{l,a,b,c\}}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{s{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha}}\times{\mathcal{D}}_{0}^{W}\times(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{{\mathsf{L}}_{\beta}s^{\prime}},

where
{\mathcal{D}}_{0}^{W},{\mathcal{D}}_{1}^{W},{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
are as in (
6.17
) and (
6.18
).

6.

For
W_{j}\in\{(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{ss^{\prime}}-P_{ss^{\prime}})\}_{s,s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{K}}\setminus\{o,i\}}
or
\{(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{ss^{\prime}}\}_{s\in{\mathcal{K}}\setminus\{o,i\},s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{V}}}
, by
Lemma

4.5
, we have
\widetilde{W}_{j}=\widehat{W}_{j}+{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
, where

\displaystyle\widehat{W}_{j}

\displaystyle=W_{j}+\frac{1}{(d-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]\atop{\mathsf{L}}\in\{l,a,b,c\}}{\mathcal{D}}_{1}^{W}\times(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha}s^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(d-1)^{2\ell}}\sum_{\alpha,\beta\in[\![{\mu}]\!]\atop{\mathsf{L}},{\mathsf{L}}^{\prime}\in\{l,a,b,c\}}(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{s{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha}}\times{\mathcal{D}}_{0}^{W}\times(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{{\mathsf{L}}_{\beta}s^{\prime}}.

7.

For
W_{j}\in\{G_{uv},G_{vv},1/G_{uu}\}_{(u,v)\in{\mathcal{V}}}
, by
Lemma

4.5
, we have, for
w\in\{u,v\}
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}_{j}=\widehat{W}_{j}+{\mathcal{D}}_{0}^{W}\times U^{(u,v)}+{\mathcal{E}}_{2},\quad\widehat{W}_{j}:=W_{j}

where
U^{(u,v)}
consists of at least two Green’s function factors in the form
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha}u},(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha}v}
, and some factors in the form
G_{vv},G_{uv},1/G_{uu}
.
Here
{\mathcal{D}}_{0}^{W},{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
are as in (
6.17
) and (
6.18
).

8.

For
W_{j}=Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)
, by
Lemma

4.6
, then
\widetilde{W}_{j}=\widehat{W}_{j}+{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
if
\widehat{W}_{j}
is a linear combination of terms in the form

\displaystyle Q-Y_{\ell}(Q),\quad\frac{1}{N}\sum_{u,v}A_{uv}(1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(Q)){\mathcal{D}}_{0}^{W}\times U^{(u,v)},

where
U^{(u,v)}
consists of at least two Green’s function factors in the form
G_{{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha}u},G_{{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha}v}
, and some factors in the form
G_{vv},G_{uv},1/G_{uu}
; Here
{\mathcal{D}}_{0}^{W},{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
are as in (
6.17
) and (
6.18
).

9.

For
W_{j}\in\{Q-m_{\rm sc},1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(Q)\}
, we have by Lemma
4.5

\widetilde{W}_{j}=\widehat{W}_{j}+{\mathcal{E}}_{3},\quad\widehat{W}_{j}:=W_{j},

where
{\mathcal{E}}_{3}
is as defined in (
6.18
).

In all the above cases, we have that
|\widetilde{W}_{j}|,|\widehat{W}_{j}|\lesssim 1
provided that
{\mathcal{G}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega(z)
. In
Item

5
and
Item

6
with
s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{V}}
, each term of
\widehat{W}_{j}
contains a term
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha}s^{\prime}}
. In particular the number of terms in the form
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{ss^{\prime}}
with
s\in{\mathcal{K}}^{+},s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{V}}
does not decrease. Thus the total number of Green’s function factors associated with each special edge does not decrease.
In
Item

9
, each term
U^{(u,v)}
contains at least two terms in the form
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{ss^{\prime}}
for
s\in{\mathcal{K}}^{+},s^{\prime}\in\{u,v\}
.

Next we show that we can replace
\widetilde{W}_{j}
by
\widehat{W}_{j}
, and the errors are negligible. Starting from (
6.14
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{Z_{\widehat{\mathcal{F}}}}\sum_{\widehat{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)(\widetilde{G}_{oo}^{(i)}-Y_{\ell}(Q))R^{\prime}_{\bf i}(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\right]\\
&=\frac{1}{(d-1)^{2\ell}Z_{\widehat{\mathcal{F}}}}\sum_{\widehat{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)\widehat{W}_{0}\prod_{j}\widehat{W}_{j}\right]\\
&+\frac{1}{(d-1)^{2\ell}Z_{\widehat{\mathcal{F}}}}\sum_{\widehat{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)\sum_{k}\prod_{j<k}\widetilde{W}_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{k}-\widehat{W}_{k})\prod_{j>k}\widehat{W}_{j}\right].\end{split}

(6.21)

To show that (
6.21
) is negligible, we prove that for each
k
,

\frac{1}{(d-1)^{2\ell}Z_{\widehat{\mathcal{F}}}}\sum_{\widehat{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)\left|\prod_{j<k}\widetilde{W}_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{k}-\widehat{W}_{k})\prod_{j>k}\widehat{W}_{j}\right|\right]=\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}].

(6.22)

If
W_{k}
is as in
Item

1
or
Item

2
, we can first sum over the indices in
\widehat{\bf i}
corresponding to the special edges. For any special edge
(u,v)\in{\mathcal{V}}
, there are at least two factors of the form
(d-1)^{2\ell}\widetilde{G}_{sw}
or
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{sw}
where
s\in{\mathcal{K}},w\in\{u,v\}
. The summation over
u,v
in (
6.22
), by the Ward identity, we have

\displaystyle\frac{(d-1)^{2\ell}}{N}\sum_{w\in[\![{N}]\!]}|G_{sw}|^{2},\frac{(d-1)^{2\ell}}{N}\sum_{w\in[\![{N}]\!]}|\widetilde{G}_{sw}|^{2}\lesssim\Phi.

After summing over the special edges, we then sum over the indices
\widehat{\bf i}\setminus\{o,i\}
. This leads to the overall bound of

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{o,i}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}\left[I(\{o,i\},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)|{\mathcal{E}}_{1}|\left(|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|+\Phi\right)^{2p-1}\right]\right),

(6.23)

where
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
is as defined in (
6.18
).
By
Proposition

5.3
, when taking the expectation, we are left with
\OO(\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}])
. If
W_{k}
is as in
Item

3
or
Item

4
, a similar argument implies that the total error is negligible.

If
W_{k}
is as in
Item

5
,
Item

6
, or
Item

7
, similarly we can first sum over the special edges, then the remaining indices in
\widehat{\bf i}
. We notice that if the term
W_{k}
is the Green’s function involving the special edges, then the summation has one less factor of
\Phi
. But in these cases we have a better bound
|{\mathcal{E}}_{2}|=N^{-2}
(recall from (
6.18
)), and the final bound is given as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)\left(|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|+\Phi\right)^{2p-2}\right]=\OO(\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}]).

(6.24)

If
W_{k}
is as in
Item

8
, similarly we can first sum over the special edges, and then sum over the remaining indices in
\widehat{\bf i}
. In this case, we have one less copy of
Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)
factor, but
|{\mathcal{E}}_{2}|=N^{-2}
(recall from (
6.18
))

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)\left(|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|+\Phi\right)^{2p-2}\right]=\OO(\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}]).

(6.25)

If
W_{k}
is as in
Item

9
, similarly we can first sum over the special edges and then over the remaining indices in
\widehat{\bf i}
, together with
|{\mathcal{E}}_{3}|=\Phi
from (
6.18
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)\Phi\left(|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|+\Phi\right)^{2p-1}\right]=\OO(\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}]).

(6.26)

The claim (
6.22
) follows from combining (
6.23
), (
6.24
), (
6.26
) and (
6.25
).

We remark that in the above procedure, each time if we replace some
W_{j}=Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)
by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{u,v}A_{uv}(1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(Q)){\mathcal{D}}_{0}^{W}\times U^{(u,v)}

(6.27)

by using
Item

8
, this gives a new special edge
(u,v)
. We denote the set of these new special edges
\{\widehat{e}_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\widehat{\mu}}]\!]}
, and denote

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}^{+}=({\bf i}^{+},E^{+})=\widehat{\mathcal{F}}\bigcup_{\alpha\in[\![{\widehat{\mu}}]\!]}\{\widehat{e}_{\alpha}\},\quad{\mathcal{V}}^{+}={\mathcal{V}}\cup\{\widehat{e}_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\widehat{\mu}}]\!]}.

Each special edge
(u,v)
is associated with at least two factors in the form
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{ss^{\prime}}
with
s\in{\mathcal{K}}^{+}
and
s^{\prime}\in\{u,v\}
, and a factor
1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(Q)
. Moreover the total number of special edges, plus the factors of the form
(Q-Y_{\ell}(Q))
or
\overline{(Q-Y_{\ell}(Q))}
is
2p-1
at every step.

From the above discussion, we can rewrite the second line in (
6.21
) as,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d-1)^{2\ell}Z_{\widehat{\mathcal{F}}}}\sum_{\widehat{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)\prod_{j}\widehat{W}_{j}\right]=\frac{1}{(d-1)^{2\ell}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)\prod_{j}\widehat{W}_{j}\right],

(6.28)

where we replaced the indicator function
I(\widehat{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})
by
I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})
, which include the special edges, and
Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}=Z_{\widehat{\mathcal{F}}}(Nd)^{\widehat{\mu}}
. Then we can further rewrite (
6.28
)
as a weighted sum of terms with the total weight bounded by
\OO(\poly(\ell))
, in the form

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{(d-1)^{2\ell}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}\right],\\
&\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}=\frac{1}{(d-1)^{(k_{1}+k_{2}/2+k_{3}/2)\ell}}\sum_{\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma}}\prod_{j=1}^{k_{1}}A_{\alpha_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{k_{2}}B_{\beta_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{k_{3}/2}C_{\gamma_{2j-1}\gamma_{2j}}D(\bm{\theta}),\end{split}

(6.29)

Here the summation for
\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma}
is over each
\alpha_{j},\beta_{j},\gamma_{j}
in one of the sets
[\![{\mu}]\!]
,
{\mathsf{A}}_{i}=\{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]:\dist_{\mathcal{T}}(i,o)=\ell+1\}
and
[\![{\mu}]\!]\setminus{\mathsf{A}}_{i}
;
and the factors in (
6.29
) are given by

\displaystyle A_{\alpha}=(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q),

\displaystyle B_{\beta}\in\left\{(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{c_{\beta}c}^{(b_{\beta}b)},(d-1)^{\ell}G_{b_{\beta}s^{\prime}},(d-1)^{\ell}G_{c_{\beta}s^{\prime}}\right\},\quad(b,c)\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ},\quad s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{K}}\cup{\mathcal{V}}\setminus\{o,i\}

\displaystyle C_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}=(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{c_{\gamma}c_{\gamma^{\prime}}}^{(b_{\gamma}b_{\gamma^{\prime}})}

\displaystyle D(\bm{\theta})\text{ is a product of other terms},

where
D(\bm{\theta})
indicates that it depends on
\{b_{\theta},c_{\theta}\}_{\theta\in\bm{\theta}}
for some
{\bm{\theta}}\subset[\![{\mu}]\!]
.

Next we show that if any index in
\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta}
appears only once among
\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma},\bm{\theta}
, then

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{(d-1)^{2\ell}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}\right]\right|\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}].

(6.30)

There are two cases

1.

Assume
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
contains one of the terms
(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)
,
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{c_{\alpha}c}^{(b_{\alpha}b)}
, or
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha}s^{\prime}}
, where
s^{\prime}
does not belong to a special edge, and the index
\alpha
only appears once. Then we can first average over the resampling edge
\{(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\}
. Thanks to
Proposition

6.1
, this gives a factor
\OO(N^{-1+{\mathfrak{c}}})
. Next we can sum over the special edges, and then sum over the remaining indices in
{\bf i}^{+}
, to conclude that

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\left|\frac{1}{(d-1)^{2\ell}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{1-{\mathfrak{c}}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\left(|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|+\Phi\right)^{2p-1}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}].

2.

If
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
contains a term
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha}u}
, where
(u,v)
is a special edge, and the index
\alpha
only appears once. Then
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
contains at least one other Green’s function term in the form
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{ss^{\prime}}
with
s\in{\mathcal{K}}^{+}
and
s^{\prime}\in\{u,v\}
. If there are more than one such terms, the same argument as in the first case leads to the desired bound.
Otherwise, we can first sum over special edges
{\mathcal{V}}^{+}\setminus\{u,v\}
, and then sum over the remaining indices in
{\bf i}^{+}\setminus\{s,u,v\}
, to conclude that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{(d-1)^{2\ell}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}\right]\\
&=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{s,u,v}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)I(s,{\mathcal{G}})A_{uv}(1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(Q))(d-1)^{2\ell}\mathbb{E}_{\bf S}[G_{{\mathsf{L}}_{\alpha}u}](d-1)^{2\ell}G_{ss^{\prime}}w_{s}U^{(u,v)}\right],\end{split}

(6.31)

where for
{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega
,
|U^{(u,v)}|\lesssim 1
(which is a product of
G_{vv},G_{uv},1/G_{uu}
), and the vector
w\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
satisfies

\displaystyle|w_{s}|\lesssim\left(|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|+\Phi\right)^{2p-2},\quad 1\leqslant s\leqslant N.

Thanks to
Proposition

6.1
, we can further upper bound (
6.31
) as

\displaystyle|\eqref{l:single_index2}|\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\frac{|1-Y^{\prime}_{\ell}(Q)|}{N\eta}\Phi\left(|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|+\Phi\right)^{2p-2}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\Phi_{p}],

as desired.

From the discussion above, we can focus on the case that each index in
\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta}
appears at least twice among
\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma},\bm{\theta}
. For the indices
\bm{\gamma}
, there are three cases:

1.

If
k_{3}/2\geqslant 2
, then for fixed
\bm{\gamma},\bm{\theta}
, the total number of new indices for the summation of
\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta}
is at most
(k_{1}+k_{2})/2
. Consequently, (
6.29
) is a weighted sum of terms with total weights
\OO(1)
, in the form

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d-1)^{{(k_{1}-k_{3})}\ell/2}}\prod_{j=1}^{k_{1}}A_{\alpha_{i}}\prod_{j=1}^{k_{2}}B_{\beta_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{k_{3}/2}C_{\gamma_{2j-1}\gamma_{2j}}D(\bm{\theta})=:\frac{1}{(d-1)^{(k_{1}-k_{3})\ell/2}}R_{{\bf i}^{+}},

(6.32)

Since
k_{3}/2\geqslant 2
,
Proposition

5.4
implies that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d-1)^{2\ell+(k_{1}-k_{3})\ell/2}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)R_{{\bf i}^{+}}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}].

2.

If
k_{3}/2\leqslant 1
and each index in
\bm{\gamma}
appears at least twice among
\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma},\bm{\theta}
.
Then for fixed
\bm{\theta}
, the total number of new indices for the summation of
\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma}
is at most
(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3})/2
. Consequently, (
6.29
) is a weighted sum of terms with total weights
\OO(1)
, in the form

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d-1)^{(k_{1}+k)\ell/2}}\prod_{j=1}^{k_{1}}A_{\alpha_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{k_{2}}B_{\beta_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{k_{3}/2}C_{\gamma_{2j-1}\gamma_{2j}}D(\bm{\theta})=:\frac{1}{(d-1)^{(k_{1}+k)\ell/2}}R_{{\bf i}^{+}},

(6.33)

where the total number of new indices in
\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma}
is
(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}-k)/2
with
k\geqslant 0
, meaning that (
6.28
) is a weighted sum of terms (with total weights
\OO(\poly(\ell))
) in form

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d-1)^{2\ell+(k_{1}+k)\ell/2}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)R_{{\bf i}^{+}}\right].

(6.34)

3.

If
k_{3}/2\leqslant 1
and
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
contains a term
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{c_{\nu}c_{\nu^{\prime}}}^{(b_{\nu}b_{\nu^{\prime}})}
, where
\nu\neq\nu^{\prime}\in\bm{\gamma}\cup\bm{\theta}
and the index
\nu
only appears once.
Then we can first average over the resampling edge
\{(b_{\nu},c_{\nu})\}
, using (
6.2
). In this way, we can replace
G_{c_{\nu}c_{\nu^{\prime}}}^{(b_{\nu}b_{\nu^{\prime}})}
by

\displaystyle(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{c_{\nu}c_{\nu}}^{(b_{\nu})}-Q)G_{b_{\nu}c_{\nu^{\prime}}}^{(b_{\nu^{\prime}})}=(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{c_{\nu}c_{\nu}}^{(b_{\nu})}-Q)\left(G_{b_{\nu}c_{\nu^{\prime}}}-\frac{G_{b_{\nu}b_{\nu^{\prime}}}G_{b_{\nu^{\prime}}c_{\nu^{\prime}}}}{G_{b_{\nu^{\prime}}b_{\nu^{\prime}}}}\right),

(6.35)

and the error terms are bounded by
\OO(\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}])
thanks to
Proposition

5.1
and
Proposition

5.4
. Condition on the event that
I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})=1
, we can further expand the last term in (
6.35
)

\displaystyle\frac{G_{b_{\nu}b_{\nu^{\prime}}}G_{b_{\nu^{\prime}}c_{\nu^{\prime}}}}{G_{b_{\nu^{\prime}}b_{\nu^{\prime}}}}=\frac{G_{b_{\nu}b_{\nu^{\prime}}}}{m_{d}}\sum_{k=1}^{{\mathfrak{b}}}\frac{(-1)^{k}(G_{b_{\nu}^{\prime}b_{\nu}^{\prime}}-P_{b_{\nu}^{\prime}b_{\nu}^{\prime}})^{k}(m_{\rm sc}+(G_{b_{\nu^{\prime}}c_{\nu^{\prime}}}-P_{b_{\nu^{\prime}}c_{\nu^{\prime}}}))}{m_{d}^{k}}+\OO(N^{-2}),

(6.36)

for
{\mathfrak{b}}\geqslant 1
large enough. Thanks to
Theorem

2.4
, the error in the above expansion can be made smaller then
\OO(N^{-2})
.
The same as in the (
6.32
), using (
6.35
) and (
6.36
), up to an error
\OO(\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}])
, we can write (
6.29
) as a weighted sum of terms with total weights
\OO(1)
, in the form

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d-1)^{(k_{1}-k_{3})\ell/2}}(G_{c_{\nu}c_{\nu}}^{(b_{\nu})}-Q)R^{\prime}_{{\bf i}^{+}},

(6.37)

In particular, (
6.28
) is a weighted sum of terms (with total weights
\OO(\poly(\ell))
) in form

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d-1)^{(k_{1}-k_{3}+4)\ell/2}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)(G_{c_{\nu}c_{\nu}}^{(b_{\nu})}-Q)R^{\prime}_{{\bf i}^{+}}\right],

(6.38)

where
k_{3}/2\leqslant 1
.

Proof of
Proposition

3.10
.

There are several cases, for (
6.37
) and (
6.33
) depending on the choice when we replace
\widetilde{G}_{oo}^{(i)}-Y_{\ell}(Q)

by using
Item

1
,

1.

For (
6.37
), we can further replace
Q
by
Y_{\ell}(Q)
, and the error is negligible

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{(d-1)^{(k_{1}-k_{3}+4)\ell/2}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)(G_{c_{\nu}c_{\nu}}^{(b_{\nu})}-Q)R_{{\bf i}^{+}}^{\prime}\right]\\
&=\frac{1}{(d-1)^{(k_{1}-k_{3}+4)\ell/2}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)(G_{c_{\nu}c_{\nu}}^{(b_{\nu})}-Y_{\ell}(Q))R_{{\bf i}^{+}}^{\prime}\right]\\
&+\OO\left(\frac{1}{(d-1)^{\ell/2}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)|Y_{\ell}(Q)-Q||R_{{\bf i}^{+}}^{\prime}|\right]\right),\end{split}

(6.39)

where we used that
k_{1}-k_{3}+4\geqslant 1
.

For the righthand side of (
6.39
), by the same argument as in the proof of
Proposition

3.9
, up to a negligible error
\OO(\mathbb{E}[\Phi_{p}])
, we can replace
\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)
back to
\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)
.Then (
5.27
) gives that the last error term in (
6.39
) is bounded by
\mathbb{E}[\Phi_{p}]
.
For the first term on the righthand side of (
6.39
), if in
R_{{\bf i}^{+}}
the total number of special edges,
Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)
and its complex conjugate is exactly
2p-1
, we get (
3.16
) by noticing that
k_{1}-k_{3}+4\geqslant 1
. Otherwise if the total number is at least
2p
, thanks to (
5.27
)

\displaystyle|\eqref{e:R+term}|\lesssim(d-1)^{-\ell}\mathbb{E}[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)(|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|+\Phi)^{2p}]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}].

2.

If
k_{1}\geqslant 1
in (
6.33
), we can rewrite (
6.33
) as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d-1)^{(k_{1}+k)\ell/2}}R_{\bf i}^{+}=:\frac{1}{(d-1)^{(k_{1}+k)\ell/2}}(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)R_{{\bf i}^{+}}^{\prime}.

Then we can process as in the previous case
Item

1
, which leads to (
3.16
).

3.

If (
6.33
) contains two terms in the form
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{cc^{\prime}}^{(bb^{\prime})}
with
(b,c)\neq(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})\in({\mathcal{C}}^{+})^{\circ}
; or one term in the form
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{cc^{\prime}}^{(bb^{\prime})}
, and one Green’s function term
(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{ss^{\prime}}-P_{ss^{\prime}})
with
s,s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{K}}^{+}
but in different connected components of
{\mathcal{F}}^{+}
; or one term in the form
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{cc^{\prime}}^{(bb^{\prime})}
and
{\mathcal{V}}^{+}\neq\emptyset
, then
Proposition

5.4
implies that

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{(d-1)^{2\ell}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)\frac{1}{(d-1)^{(k_{1}+k)\ell/2}}R_{{\bf i}^{+}}\right]=\OO(\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}]).

4.

If
k_{3}=2
in (
6.33
), then (
6.33
) contains
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{c_{\beta}c_{\beta^{\prime}}}^{(b_{\beta}b_{\beta^{\prime}})}
where the indices
\beta,\beta^{\prime}
appears at least twice among
\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma},\bm{\theta}
in (
6.33
) (otherwise, we are in the case for (
6.37
)). If
k_{1}\geqslant 1
, this is covered in
Item

2
. In the following we assume that
k_{1}=0
,
{\mathcal{V}}^{+}=\emptyset
and (
6.33
) does not contain other terms in the form
(d-1)^{2\ell}G^{bb^{\prime}}_{cc^{\prime}}
with
(b,c)\neq(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})\in({\mathcal{C}}^{+})^{\circ}
, or
(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{ss^{\prime}}-P_{ss^{\prime}})
with
s,s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{K}}^{+}
but in different connected components of
{\mathcal{F}}^{+}
. Otherwise it has been covered in
Item

2
and
Item

3
.
Thus in (
6.33
),

\displaystyle k_{1}=0,\quad k_{2}=0,\quad k_{3}=2.

Moreover, all the factors in (
6.33
) come from
Item

1
,
Item

2
,
Item

4
,
W_{j}
in
Item

6
,
W_{j}
in
Item

8
, and
Item

9
. We recall that the indices
\beta,\beta^{\prime}
appear at least twice among
\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma},\bm{\theta}
. It follows that
{\mathsf{D}}(\bm{\theta})
contains
(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{c_{\beta}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\beta})}-Q)
and
(d-1)^{2\ell}(G^{(b_{\beta^{\prime}})}_{c_{\beta^{\prime}}c_{\beta^{\prime}}}-Q)
.
Thus
k=2
in (
6.33
), and by the same argument as in
Item

1
, (
6.34
) leads to (
3.16
).

5.

Finally we discuss the case that
\widetilde{W}_{0}
is replaced by
{\mathcal{E}}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}^{S}
as described in (
6.16
) with
r\geqslant 2
.
If
{\mathcal{E}}
contains at least one term in the form
(d-1)^{2\ell}(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)
, the same argument as in
Item

1
, (
6.34
) leads to (
3.17
);
If
{\mathcal{E}}
contains at least one term in the form
(d-1)^{2\ell}G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}
, by the same argument as in
Item

4
, (
6.34
) leads to (
3.17
).

If
{\mathcal{E}}
contains at least one term in the form
(d-1)^{2\ell}(Q-Y_{\ell}(Q))
, then
|{\mathcal{E}}|\lesssim(d-1)^{2\ell}|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|(d-1)^{2\ell}\varepsilon
. The total number of special edges,
Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)
and its complex conjugate is at least
2p
. It follows

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{(d-1)^{2\ell}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)\frac{1}{(d-1)^{(k_{1}+k)\ell/2}}R_{{\bf i}^{+}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}[(d-1)^{2\ell}\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\varepsilon(|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|+\Phi)^{2p}]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}].

In the remaining cases
{\mathcal{E}}
contains at least two terms in the form
(d-1)^{2\ell}(Q-m_{\rm sc})
, this leads to

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{(d-1)^{2\ell}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)\frac{1}{(d-1)^{(k_{1}+k)\ell/2}}R_{{\bf i}^{+}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}[(d-1)^{2\ell}\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|Q-m_{\rm sc}|^{2}(|Q-Y_{\ell}(Q)|+\Phi)^{2p-1}]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{p}].

The cases above enumerate all possibilities and complete the proof of
Proposition

3.10
.

∎
